## 10. `kills/README.md`'s fetch-verify caveat is unconditional and measurably false

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a yes/no.)*
Do you want the kills README's fetch-verify note corrected to say the beacon is only
injected when the fetch asks for HTML, and to record the fetch that does hash-match?

- *Why:* The README currently says the live host always injects a small analytics beacon
  into HTML pages, so a live fetch of an archived board's index page will never match the
  stored hash, and tells verifiers to use the deployment's own preview address instead. That
  was measured on 25 August, but a later measurement (reproduced by two readers and the
  orchestrator) found the injection only happens when the request carries an Accept:
  text/html header — a plain fetch returns the exact stored bytes and hash.
- *Options:*
  a. **Yes — correct the note and record the working method** — The README says the injection
     is conditional on the Accept header, and tells the next verifier to fetch without Accept:
     text/html to hash-match archived HTML directly at the live host. *Costs:* One docs edit.
     The measured fact replaces a measured-but-superseded one.
  b. **No — leave the note as written** — The README keeps its unconditional claim and its
     workaround. *Costs:* The claim is measurably false today, and the workaround is more work
     than the method that actually matches.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator's read: correct it — this is
  not a judgement call, the README states something false and the true version is measured,
  reproduced twice, and cheaper. *Yours:* Only the yes; the entry does not name a design
  half.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked, but this note is why the sweep that re-verified the
  heat board's archive stopped after checking only the sim file and skipped the HTML half,
  and it will stop the next one for the same false reason.

**Question.** Yes or no: correct the caveat to say the beacon injection is Accept-header
conditional, and record the working method?

**What the document says.** `kills/README.md:35-56`: *"Fetch-verify caveat, measured
2026-08-25: the durable host injects the Cloudflare Insights beacon into HTML (+359 bytes
on /dawnspur/ — 12024 live vs 11665 deployed), so a live fetch of index.html will NOT
hash-match these captures."*

**What source says.** `docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:860-869`, reproduced by both readers and by
the orchestrator: *"a plain fetch of `https://boards.skyrailreclamation.com/dawnspur/`
returns **200, 11,665 bytes, sha256 `bdde9b50331ac89d…97ac59`** — exactly beat `:13`, the
HEAD blob, `test:40` and `kills/README.md:31`. With `Accept: text/html` it returns
**12,024 / `540c9ee2…`** … The injection is triggered by the **Accept header, not the
User-Agent**."* And the conclusion: *"it removes a standing excuse: to hash-match archived
HTML at the durable host, fetch without an `Accept: text/html` header."*

**Blocked.** Nothing — but the caveat is why the CFD-176 sweeper stopped at the sim half,
and it will stop the next one.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Correct it.* This one is not a judgement: the README states a
false unconditional and the true conditional is measured, reproduced twice, and cheaper
than the workaround the README recommends.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** Every future archive verification skips the HTML half on a
false premise.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** RULED BY COUNSEL ON DAVID'S
INSTRUCTION, 2026-09-09 — option (a). Correct kills/README.md:35-39 in place: the
Cloudflare Insights injection is conditional on the request's Accept header, a plain fetch
of an archived board's index.html at the durable host returns the stored bytes and
hash-matches, and that is the method the next verifier uses. The 2026-08-25 reading is kept
beside it as measured-then, the *.pages.dev route is kept as a second route because it is
not false, and the correction publishes NO injected byte count at all — not the old one and
not either new one. *(unanimous; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one
moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in
this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named under Carried out
moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* Not a judgement call: the README states an unconditional that is measurably false,
  and the true conditional is now measured four times. docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:805-809
  records the refutation and its reproduction ('returns **200, 11,665 bytes, sha256 …' and
  'The injection is triggered by the **Accept header, not the User-Agent** (the refuter
  isolated all four combinations)') — the entry's cite of :790-799 and this council's first
  cite of :805-812 are both approximate; find it by text. All FOUR counsellors re-fetched it
  independently on 2026-09-09 — cost, skeptic, reader and canon each record a plain fetch of
  11,665 bytes hashing bdde9b50…97ac59, byte-exact to kills/README.md:31, and an Accept:
  text/html fetch of 12,032 — not three, as this council first wrote. Cheaper than the
  workaround it replaces, and the entry's own cost is real: the sweep records that this
  caveat is why the CFD-176 sweeper graded the live kill UNCHECKABLE and stopped at the sim
  half. The edit is free — the moderator re-ran grep over test/ for 'kills/README', 'Insights
  beacon' and '12024' and it returns nothing, no hash pins the file, and it lives outside
  every board directory. The one thing all four re-measurements added, and the ruling adopts:
  the injected payload is NOT a constant — the injected size read 12,024 on the earlier dates
  and 12,032 on 2026-09-09 — and, corrected at revision, every one of those readings came
  from ONE machine on ONE network path, with the skeptic's own caveat that the +8 may be a
  header-size artefact of its proxy rather than a change at the host. Publishing any of the
  three would repeat the 2026-08-25 mistake one level down, which is this entry's own failure
  mode. The note therefore states the method, the dates, and that the injected size is not
  stable and was never measured from a second path — and publishes no figure. That is the
  ambient contract's 'prefer null to a plausible number' and 'measurement provenance travels
  with the number'.
- *Dissent:* None on the direction; the skeptic's dissent was on form and is adopted twice
  over — the correction is a dated supersession in place that keeps the 2026-08-25 reading as
  an earlier measurement and states the new one as conditional and dated, never as a new
  unconditional; it keeps the pages.dev fallback for the day Cloudflare changes its trigger;
  and, at revision, its caveat about the single measurement path is carried into the note
  itself rather than left in the counsellor's file.
- *For David, not blocking:* None.
- *Action:* [docs] Supersede kills/README.md:35-39 in place. New text states: the injection
  is conditional on an 'Accept: text/html' request header (measured 2026-09-02, re-measured
  by four independent fetches 2026-09-09); a plain fetch returns the archived bytes and
  hash-matches kills/README.md:31 (11,665 bytes, sha256 bdde9b50…97ac59), and that is the
  verification method; the injected size is NOT stable between dates and every reading came
  from one machine and one network path, so no injected byte count and no delta is published
  here — including the +359 the file currently carries; the *.skyrail-boards.pages.dev route
  remains valid as a second route. Keep the 2026-08-25 sentence beside it, marked
  measured-then. Do not touch the two capture tables — 'Do not hand-edit' at :3 governs the
  archive record, and no script generates this file (grep over the repo finds no generator).
  (C:/dev/skyrail-boards/kills/README.md) · [record] Mark entry 10 RULED, and record that the
  +359 figure the entry quotes is superseded not by a replacement figure but by a method: the
  beacon's size is a host property that drifts, was measured only from one path, and must not
  be pinned anywhere in this repository.
  (C:/dev/skyrail-boards/docs/decisions-open-2026-09-02.md)
- *Carried out:* at `27a900c` — `kills/README.md`: the injected-size sentence corrected
  (unstable across three readings from one machine; no figure published; the 2026-08-25
  sentence kept beside it as measured-then).

## 11. The stale heat index pin `cedf765c` still ships, in four places

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: pick one of two or three.)*
Do you want the out-of-date heat-index hash fixed everywhere it appears — including inside
the shipped manifest of the passed scale board — or only in the two beat copies?

- *Why:* The scale board's manifest records the live heat board's index hash, but the value
  it holds is the heat index as it stood between the two greenhouse commits — the second one
  changed the index page again and this line was never updated; the heat board's own manifest
  has the correct current value. The same stale value sits in two places in the scale beat
  with no note, while two other mentions in that same beat already carry a note saying it was
  replaced; the audit only noticed one of the four sites.
- *Options:*
  a. **Yes — fix all four** — Re-pin the hash in the scale board's manifest (both the
     archived and the served copy) and add notes to the two unmarked beat mentions. *Costs:* The
     manifest is part of a passed board's bytes; editing it breaks the lineage rule that a
     passed board's bytes are frozen.
  b. **The orchestrator's split — fix the two beat copies now, leave the manifest and record
     why** — The two beat mentions get the same note their neighbours already have; the scale
     manifest keeps its stale line, with a note elsewhere explaining that it is known wrong and
     left because the board passed as those bytes. *Costs:* A shipped manifest keeps
     misdescribing what it pins, which the orchestrator's own read admits is worse than a beat
     typo.
  c. **No — leave all four** — Nothing changes. *Costs:* Two free docs fixes go unmade and
     the manifest stays wrong.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator's read: fix the two beat copies
  now and leave the manifest bytes with a recorded reason, because editing a passed board's
  shipped bytes to fix a comment is exactly the trade the lineage rule exists to refuse.
  *Yours:* Whether the frozen-bytes rule for a passed board covers its manifest's comment
  lines, when the comment is known to be wrong.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked. But a future check of the live heat board against the
  scale manifest will fail against a value that was already out of date before the scale beat
  was signed, and be read as the heat board having drifted.

**Question.** Yes or no: re-pin the heat index in the shipped MANIFEST of a passed board,
and supersede the two remaining stale copies in `cfd-183`?

**What the document says.** `sit/dawnspur-scale/MANIFEST.txt:25`: *"live /dawnspur-heat/
build c887359 / index cedf765c / sim 292d6645 / greenhouse 7fdf7468"*.

**What source says.** Measured at HEAD: `sha256sum sit/dawnspur-heat/index.html` is
`b5f7e14f4ed82a81…bba995`, and `sit/dawnspur-heat/MANIFEST.txt:19` records exactly that.
`cedf765c` was the heat index at `c887359` and was superseded by `efbed23`. **The stale
value survives in four files**, not the one the audit named:
`sit/dawnspur-scale/MANIFEST.txt:25`, `public/dawnspur-scale/MANIFEST.txt:25` (so it is
served), and `docs/cfd-183-beat.md:329` and `:385` — both **unsuperseded** *(measured 2026-09-02; on 2026-09-09 both received the note, carried out under counsel's ruling on entry 9 — see the ruling below)*, while the same
pin at `:45` and `:182` in the same file *does* carry supersession notes from the
2026-09-01 sweep.

**Raised as.** `docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:968-971` — *"**The same stale pin
is shipped** in `sit/dawnspur-scale/MANIFEST.txt:25` — a passed board's bytes; David's."*
The audit named one of four sites.

**Blocked.** Nothing. The two `docs/` copies are free; the two MANIFEST copies are `sit/`
and `public/` bytes on a passed board, which the lineage rule protects.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Supersede the two `docs/` copies now; leave the MANIFEST bytes
and record why.* A MANIFEST that misdescribes what it pins is worse than a docs typo, but
editing a passed board's shipped bytes to fix a comment is exactly the trade the lineage
rule exists to refuse.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** A future fetch-verify of `/dawnspur-heat/` fails against a
pin that was never right, and is read as the board having drifted.

**RULED IN PART — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09; the rest RETURNED TO
DAVID.** INTERIM RULING BY COUNSEL ON DAVID'S INSTRUCTION, 2026-09-09 — option (b), the
split. Supersede the two unsuperseded docs copies now (docs/cfd-183-beat.md:329 and :385)
in the form :45 and :182 already carry, and leave sit/dawnspur-scale/MANIFEST.txt:25 and
its byte-copy at public/dawnspur-scale/MANIFEST.txt:25 as passed bytes, with the defect
recorded where §7.7 says status lives — in the beat and in the test. Option (a)'s manifest
half is REQUIRES DAVID. *(majority; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic —
one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in
this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named under Carried out
moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* The council was handed a rule it may not overturn: an option that edits a passed
  board's shipped bytes is not the council's, and /dawnspur-scale/ is 'live — passed its sit'
  with MANIFEST.txt byte-pinned sit-to-public by test/dawnspur-scale.test.js:92-100. That
  handed-down rule is the whole of the authority for the interim, and at revision the council
  states plainly what it first leaned on and should not have: canon §7.7 does NOT cleanly
  govern this line. Its worked instance (:1043-1048) is a manifest describing its OWN pin
  status — 'the file is inside the pinned directory, so neither can ever be corrected' — and
  its obligations list at :1055-1056 puts THIS line in the opposite category: 'A manifest
  records the three files' shas, and the live shas of the boards left standing. That is what
  it is for and it stays true forever.' The stale value at :25 is exactly a neighbouring
  board's live sha, the category §7.7 asserts is permanently true, and this case refutes
  'stays true forever'. Measured at e226a9f by the moderator: cedf765c survives in exactly
  four unsuperseded places — sit/ and public/dawnspur-scale/MANIFEST.txt:25 (diff clean,
  identical) and docs/cfd-183-beat.md:329 and :385 — while :45 and :182 already carry the
  note; sha256 of sit/dawnspur-heat/index.html is b5f7e14f4ed82a81…bba995, pinned in
  test/dawnspur-scale.test.js:80 and recorded in sit/dawnspur-heat/MANIFEST.txt. The two docs
  fixes are uncontested and free.
- *Dissent:* The reader's, and it is the strongest dissent in this set. It rests on the
  repository's own words: test/boards-index.test.js:43-45 excludes MANIFEST.txt from the
  graded set because 'it is a record about the board, not a shipped runtime file, and its own
  copies may legitimately differ' — the moderator opened the file and the comment reads
  exactly so — and the hub card pins index.html and sim.js only. On that reading a dated
  supersession of line 25, applied identically to both copies, overwrites no board. It loses
  on authority, not on merit: the doctrine handed to this council freezes a passed board's
  shipped bytes and routes an edit to them to David. Its second point survives into the
  carry-out and is the reason for the test pin: option (b) puts the truth in the two places
  the verifier of that line is not looking.
- *Requires David:* Do you rule that sit/dawnspur-scale/MANIFEST.txt:25 and its byte-copy
  at public/dawnspur-scale/MANIFEST.txt:25 may be re-pinned from index cedf765c to b5f7e14f —
  an edit to a passed board's shipped bytes — or does the freeze govern, with the correction
  recorded in the beat and the test instead? FOUR facts for the answer. (1) The value was
  already wrong on the day the scale beat was signed (cedf765c was the heat index before
  efbed23), not falsified later. (2) No hash pins MANIFEST.txt itself, and both scale tests
  stay green if the line changes, so the freeze here is doctrine and not a gate. (3)
  test/boards-index.test.js:43-45 already declares MANIFEST.txt 'a record about the board,
  not a shipped runtime file'. (4) Added at revision, and the one you most need: canon §7.7
  has no remedy for this line, because it did not believe one could exist. Its obligations
  say a manifest's record of 'the live shas of the boards left standing … stays true forever'
  (:1055-1056); this line is that category and it went stale. Whichever way you rule, §7.7's
  bullet needs a dated supersession saying so.
- *Action:* [docs] Supersede docs/cfd-183-beat.md:329 and :385 in the exact form :45 and
  :182 carry — the sentence restated to current fact with the earlier wording kept in a dated
  parenthetical ('An earlier cut read "`c887359` / `cedf765c` / `292d6645`"; `cedf765c` was
  the heat index before CFD-175's `efbed23`…'), naming the current value b5f7e14f. The :329
  note additionally records that sit/ and public/dawnspur-scale/MANIFEST.txt:25 carry the
  same stale value, that they are left as passed bytes under the handed-down freeze, and that
  the authority for the live heat index is sit/dawnspur-heat/MANIFEST.txt and
  test/dawnspur-scale.test.js:80. (C:/dev/skyrail-boards/docs/cfd-183-beat.md) · [test] Add a

  re-pinned, this pin goes red and is removed in the same commit. Two carry-out constraints

  reverting immediately — never commit the mutation. Second, the assertion it makes is a
  known-false string about the world, so mutate BOTH copies or neither:
  test/dawnspur-scale.test.js:92-100 pins sit/public byte-identity across MANIFEST.txt, so a
  half-applied mutation goes red for the wrong reason and reads as a drift. Note for the

## 12. Canon grades `/dawnspur-heat/`'s WARM two ways, eleven lines apart

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a design call.)*
Is the heat board's WARM a true breach of the rule that heat and air must be separate
systems (as your own collapse table in canon says), or does it survive that rule (as
canon's judgement of the board, a few lines later, says)?

- *Why:* Canon carries your 25 August collapse table, verified, and one row convicts "WARM
  as a ground step that is also a destination job" as a true collapse of rule R9 (heat that
  warms ground already reached must not also be the thing that opens new ground). Canon's
  judgement of the same board, in the next section, says its bank-in-the-stone and
  step-onto-ground survive R8/R9; the heat beat sides with the second reading while its own
  description has one WARM doing both things R9 forbids, so the same bytes are graded both
  ways in one file.
- *Options:*
  a. **A true R9 collapse** — The collapse-table row stands; canon's later sentence about
     surviving R8/R9 is corrected. The board still stands as played and passed — this is about
     what the precedent means, not about recutting. *Costs:* Every later board reads R9 the
     strict way: one act may not both warm reached ground and put a step on new ground.
  b. **Survives R8/R9** — The judgement sentence stands; the collapse-table row is corrected
     or qualified. The heat beat's own reading is confirmed. *Costs:* Every later board reads R9
     the looser way, with the heat board as the precedent for what is allowed.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator's read: none — both readings
  describe the same bytes, measurement cannot choose between them, and this is a judgement
  about what R9 means. *Yours:* All of it: what R9 forbids, adjudicated on the one board it
  has been applied to.
- *If undecided:* Every later board inherits whichever reading of R9 its author happens to
  open first, and the heat board is the precedent either way. It also holds up the question
  of adding canon-check sections to older beats: writing "Heat vs Air — R9" as a citation on
  the heat beat would silently pick the survives reading over the collapse row.
- *What the entry does not say:* The entry says this blocks "#25"; the ledger's canon-check
  retroactivity question is entry 20, and entry 25 is about a different beat. The description
  matches entry 20.

**Question.** Pick one: is the heat board's WARM a **TRUE R9 collapse** (`:105`), or does
it **survive R8/R9** (`:167-168`)?

**What the document says.** Two lines in one file. `docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:105`:
*"| Heat vs Air: WARM as ground step that is also a dest job | TRUE collapse | R9 |"*.
`:167-168`: *"Its bank-in-the-stone and step-onto-ground survive R8/R9 readings."*

**What source says.** `docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:945-951`: *"the beat's own `:19` has one
WARM doing both things R9 forbids. **This is not resolvable by measurement and must not be
averaged.**"* R9 itself is at `:84-85` at HEAD (the sweep cited `:78-81`): *"**R9 — Heat ≠
Air ≠ Growth (directive 1.19).** 'Heat that warms the ground already reached against Air
that opens new ground …'"*

**Blocked.** The canon-check retroactivity call (#20 *— corrected 2026-09-09; this read "#25", which is a different beat*) — the sweep flags that adding *"Heat
vs Air — R9"* as a citation on `cfd-176` would silently pick `:118` over `:105`.

**Orchestrator's read.** *No read.* This is a canon self-contradiction on a board that has
already passed and stands as played; measurement confirms both readings describe the same
bytes and cannot choose between them. It is a judgement about what R9 means.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** Every later board inherits the wrong reading of R9, and the
one board it was adjudicated on is the precedent.

**RULED IN PART — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09; the rest RETURNED TO
DAVID.** The heat board survives R8/R9 and the section 3 judgement at
docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:127 stands. R9's line is REACH, not warmth: Air opens ground
beyond the rim, across a cold gap; holding AND pushing ground the player can already reach
is Heat's own job. The passed board's step goes onto unsecured ground already on the island
it holds, so it is Heat's push, and no build does both. The section 2 collapse-table row at
:111 is QUALIFIED in place by one dated note beneath the table, its verdict cell left
verbatim. The qualification does NOT assert which object David's row was written against,
because that is not recoverable from this repository. The ruling holds under either
candidate referent, which is why it can be given at all. Rewriting or deleting the verdict
cell of David's own row is not the council's; that half is returned to him. *(majority;
four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial
check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree
the council read; the landing named under Carried out moved some of them.)*

## 13. Canon `:106`'s phase-lock order is not the order the board enforces

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a yes/no.)*
Do you want canon's phase-lock order corrected from GOODS / HOLD / B / WARM to GOODS, then
B, then HOLD, then WARM, which is what the heat board actually enforces?

- *Why:* Canon's collapse table writes the heat board's phase lock in one order; the
  board's own source states and enforces a different one, and driving all 24 possible
  orderings of the four commits reaches the banked state in exactly one — the board's. Both
  readers agreed the slip is canon's, not the beat's, and it was outside the sweep's scope to
  fix.
- *Options:*
  a. **Yes — correct canon's order** — Two letters swap in the canon table so it matches the
     enforced order. *Costs:* One docs edit; nothing else moves.
  b. **No — leave it** — Canon keeps the wrong order; any beat citing that row must quote
     canon's order as canon's and flag it, or cite the neighbouring judgement (which says only
     "a phase lock") and leave the row out. *Costs:* Every future citation carries a flag, and
     the heat beat's own row on the four-button phase lock stays routed to you instead of being
     closed.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator's read: correct it — two
  letters, uncontested by both readers, checked over the full ordering space. *Yours:* Only
  the yes; the entry does not name a design half.
- *If undecided:* Nothing directly, but the heat beat's phase-lock row stays open on your
  desk, and the risk is that a beat gets rewritten to match canon's typo.

**Question.** Yes or no: correct canon `:106` to the enforced order?

**What the document says.** `docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:106` writes the lock as
`GOODS/HOLD/B/WARM`.

**What source says.** The board enforces `GOODS → B → HOLD → WARM`
(`sit/dawnspur-heat/sim.js:4`), and BFS over all 24 orderings of the four commits reaches
`banked` in **exactly 1** — that one (`docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:815-816`). The sweep's
ruling at `:952-956`: *"**The slip is canon's, not the beat's**, and out of this sweep's
glob."*

**Blocked.** Nothing directly, but it is the reason `cfd-176:39` was routed to you rather
than superseded.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Correct it.* Two letters, uncontested by both readers, driven
over the full ordering space.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** A beat gets superseded to match canon's typo.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** RULED BY COUNSEL ON DAVID'S
INSTRUCTION, 2026-09-09 — option (a). Correct the collapse-table row to the order the board
enforces, GOODS / B / HOLD / WARM, as a supersession in place: the earlier wording
'GOODS/HOLD/B/WARM' is kept in a dated parenthetical stating that the board enforces GOODS
to B to HOLD to WARM, measured over all 24 orderings of the four commits. The row's verdict
('TRUE — the deepest one') and its rule citation (R10) are David's and are not touched. The
row is at docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:112 at HEAD, not :106 — the entry's line cite has
drifted, and the edit is made by finding the row's text, not by line number. *(majority;
four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial
check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree
the council read; the landing named under Carried out moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* Three counsellors re-derived the measurement independently, each with their own
  script against sit/dawnspur-heat/sim.js from the opening index.html:174 actually creates:
  exactly 1 of the 24 orderings reaches banked, and it is goods, B, hold, warm. The moderator
  confirmed the mechanism in the source: commitGoods sets phase 'b' (:40-45), commitB sets
  'hold' (:47-52), commitHold sets 'warm' (:56-62); the shipped header at sim.js:4 reads
  'Path to first WARM stays GOODS → B → HOLD → WARM'; test/dawnspur-heat.test.js:120 pins it
  by name. The correction is two letters in one docs line and no test reads the row — the
  canon file is out of the beats-lexicon corpus by name (test/beats-lexicon-ledger.js
  NON_BEAT_DOCS) and the only other mention in test/ is a comment.
- *Dissent:* The skeptic's, that the row may not assert an order at all — that
  'Profit/tend/buildout' maps positionally onto the first three of 'GOODS/HOLD/B/WARM'
  (profit to GOODS, tend to HOLD, buildout to B), and that a swap would break the mapping to
  fix a sequence the row never claimed. It does not survive, and at revision the council
  leads with the MEASURED refutation rather than the formal one, because the formal one ('a
  three-item left side cannot map positionally onto a four-item right side') is not decisive
  alone: B is the profit leg on this tree, not GOODS. docs/cfd-183-beat.md:238 reads
  'MOSSWAKE +3 | ON | Played number, standing; profit leg on off-board ground (R10)', and on
  the heat board commitB is the paying leg — sit/dawnspur-heat/sim.js:47-52, 'marks =
  Math.max(0, marks - 1) + 3'. So 'profit maps to GOODS' fails on the board, and the
  positional reading fails with it. The secondary point stands: every other row of §2 uses
  the arrow to separate a name from what it collapsed into, never as a positional mapping
  between two lists. What survives of the dissent is its form: the row sits under the heading
  'His row', so the letters do not move silently. That is why the ruling is the in-place form
  and not a swap — under the reading where the list names the four buttons as a set the
  correction is harmless, under the reading where it names the lock's order it removes a
  falsehood, and under both David's earlier wording is preserved verbatim beside it.
- *For David, not blocking:* None. If David states the row was never the phase order but a
  mapping of the three collapsed senses, the fix becomes a gloss naming what the list is; his

## 14. `armed` — REAL_COLLISION / MEDIUM, or BENIGN_SHARED_NAME / LOW?

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a design call.)*
Is the shared export name "armed" a real collision that should be treated as MEDIUM, or a
benign shared name that is LOW?

- *Why:* All four boards that carry "armed" use it to answer the same question — is the
  ending one act from stopping — and on all four a stopped board is always an armed one. But
  on the desk boards (line, storm) it is a success the player paid for (three paid UPs),
  while on the city boards (dice, two-ways) it is a failure suffered (one short run) and it
  gates a different set of verbs. The audit holds it at MEDIUM, its reviewer ruled LOW, a
  third reader called the split a matter of definition rather than fact, and the name ledger
  carries it as contested with both readings until you rule.
- *Options:*
  a. **REAL_COLLISION / MEDIUM** — One name for two different world-causes is a collision
     that must be resolved by renaming. *Costs:* A rename campaign across four boards whose
     bytes are frozen by hash.
  b. **BENIGN_SHARED_NAME / LOW** — The same abstraction ("ending is armed") with a different
     cause per lineage is fine to share; it is recorded, not renamed. *Costs:* The line side
     keeps a real meaning inversion with nothing defending it.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator's read: LOW, because the export
  answers one question on all four boards and a differing world-cause is what a shared
  abstraction looks like — but the split is definitional and a MEDIUM ruling is equally
  defensible. *Yours:* Whether "same question, different cause" counts as one name or two in
  this game's vocabulary.
- *If undecided:* Nothing downstream may cite a single severity for "armed" — the ledger
  says so in terms — so every document that touches it must carry both readings until you
  rule.

**Question.** Pick one severity.

**What the document says.** `docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:803-809`: *"One
reviewer adjudicates BENIGN_SHARED_NAME / LOW … The report holds REAL_COLLISION / MEDIUM …
The guard's ledger carries the row as CONTESTED with both readings and every measured
site; **nothing downstream may cite a single severity for it until David rules.**"*

**What source says.** *(As measured 2026-09-02; on 2026-09-09 the row moved to LOW under counsel's ruling on this entry, both readings kept, and the quoted note phrase is gone — see the ruling below.)* `test/lexicon-ledger.js:322-328` carries it CONTESTED at HEAD, with
both readings verbatim and the note *"the ruling is David's"*. What is measured and
uncontested (`:325`): *"both sides drive from the opening, and `stopped => armed` holds on
every board that carries it"*. (**This row moved from `:260` to `:322` at `919b6be`,
mid-task** — see the closing note.) The split is 2 success-armed (line, storm) vs 2
failure-armed (dice, two-ways) — `docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:469`.

**Raised in.** Audit §1.10 (`:431-474`), §5 do-not-average (`:799-805`), and the guard's
ledger (`test/lexicon-ledger.js:322-328`). One call, three places.

**Blocked.** Any downstream citation of a single severity — the ledger says so in terms *(said so until 2026-09-09; the clause was superseded under counsel's ruling on this entry, and the row is LOW)*.
The completeness critic calls the split *definitional, not factual*
(`docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:806-807`).

**Orchestrator's read.** *LOW.* The export answers one question on all four boards and the
world-cause differing is what a shared abstraction looks like; but the critic is right
that this is definitional, so a MEDIUM ruling is equally defensible and I would not argue
with it.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** MEDIUM triggers a rename campaign across four boards whose
bytes are hash-pinned; LOW leaves a real inversion undefended on the line side.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** RULED BY COUNSEL ON DAVID'S
INSTRUCTION, 2026-09-09 — BENIGN_SHARED_NAME / LOW. The armed row in test/lexicon-ledger.js
moves from CONTESTED to LOW; both readings stay recorded verbatim beneath the adjudication
and are not averaged; the per-side meaning cells (meaning.desk, meaning.city) and the
driven columns stay. Corrected at revision: keeping them is not self-executing, so the
ruling does not merely declare them mandatory — it adds the instrument. A named assertion
in test/lexicon.test.js requires the armed row, by name and regardless of rank, to carry a
driven or source-pinned column on every board it names; without it the mandate is a
sentence with nothing behind it, which is the exact failure this ruling uses to defeat
MEDIUM. The row's 'by' field records the ruling as by counsel on David's instruction of
2026-09-09, never in his name. *(majority; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the
skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words.
Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named
under Carried out moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* Canon settles what the word names, and it is a relation, not a valence. §7.2's
  companion ruling (docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:595-598, RULED): 'Arming-and-triggering
  (§7.2's companion in CFD-201 ruling 5 — topping the terrace *arms* the ending, the
  qualifying cargo *triggers* it)'. Armed means the ending is one act from firing. The city
  boards use that grammar in their own source — public/dice-at-the-places/sim.js:341-343:
  'The stop, stated explicitly: a turned-back run arms; Collect at Mosswake fires.' Nothing
  in R1-R10 ties the arm's cause to a success, and §7's one-NEW-system rule is what produces
  a different world-cause per lineage. The measured and uncontested fact in the ledger itself
  (test/lexicon-ledger.js:344) is that both sides drive from the opening and 'stopped =>
  armed' holds on every board that carries it, and no index.html reads the getter. The second
  reason is procedural and decisive: MEDIUM's stated remedy is a rename across four passed,
  hash-pinned boards, which neither this council nor anyone else may carry out — a severity
  that can only ever be recorded is a guard aimed at nobody who can act on it, which the
  ambient contract refuses. LOW with both readings kept is the label the next beat author can
  actually use. Checked before ruling, and re-checked at revision: no test asserts armed's
  adjudication string; test/lexicon.test.js:300-313 demands measured columns only of HIGH and
  CONTESTED rows, so LOW cannot go red on that account — and that is precisely why the
  downgrade needs its own pin. The evidence does not go dark on its own account either way:
  test/lexicon.test.js:470-477 builds one test per row that declares drives, regardless of
  rank, so the drives that EXIST keep executing; what LOW removes is the obligation to keep
  them, and the new assertion restores it for this row alone.
- *Dissent:* The skeptic's: the ledger says in terms that 'the ruling is David's', the
  orchestrator's own read concedes MEDIUM is equally defensible, and a coin-flip is not a
  consensus — so the honest state is CONTESTED until he answers. It loses on the delegation
  and on the record: David's instruction of 2026-09-09 put exactly these decisions to this
  council, and the canon lens found a hook the entry did not have — §7.2's arm/fire grammar,
  which the city boards quote verbatim — so the split is no longer a bare definitional
  coin-flip. The dissent's true residual is carried into the actions: the line side keeps a
  real meaning inversion with no reciprocal by-name pin (audit :461-462), and the trigger for
  re-opening is named.
- *For David, not blocking:* None — his 2026-09-09 instruction discharges the ledger's 'the
  ruling is David's', and the row records the ruling as by counsel on that instruction. One
  word from him flips the label back.
- *Action:* [test] In test/lexicon-ledger.js, change the armed row's adjudication from
  CONTESTED to LOW (the row is at :341-347 at HEAD: 'armed:' at :341, adjudication at :343,
  'by' at :344, the two readings at :346-347 — the entry's cite of :322-328 is stale and is

  ruling as by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09, keeping the audit and reviewer
  readings verbatim beneath it and the measured, uncontested sentence intact. Keep the
  meaning.desk and meaning.city cells and every driven column. Add the re-open trigger in one
  sentence: a board that carries armed from both lineages, or any page that reads the getter,
  makes the collision real and the row returns to MEDIUM on that board.
  (C:/dev/skyrail-boards/test/lexicon-ledger.js) · [test] Added at revision, and this is the

## 15. §7.2's "honest" test — not EV-dominated, or free-and-always-lit?

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: pick one of two or three.)*
Your rule says every board must offer a quick path that is honest — does "honest" mean the
quick path is free, always available and never locks you out, or does it mean the quick
path is not a worse deal in marks than the long one?

- *Why:* Your own rule gives the dispatch board's free, always-lit floor send as the
  example of an honest quick path, but the line board's beat passes its quick path on a
  different test — average marks per send — and a fresh measurement on that test puts the
  free Halt send behind Mosswake at every roster size. The section of canon right after this
  one says two options that differ in both marks and food cannot be reduced to one number, so
  the marks-comparison reading makes two of your rulings contradict each other.
- *Options:*
  a. **Free, always available, never locking** — "Honest" is about access: the short route
     costs nothing, is always lit, and never traps the player. A board passes if such a route
     exists, regardless of whether it pays less. *Costs:* The line board's beat, which passed
     its quick path on the marks comparison, is then graded on the wrong test and its self-check
     has to be re-read.
  b. **Not a worse deal in marks** — "Honest" is about payoff: the short route must not lose
     to the long route on average marks per send. *Costs:* On this reading the free Halt send
     already fails on every roster, and the reading needs a single-number comparison that your
     next ruling forbids — so two ruled sections would disagree.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator reads it as free, always
  available, never locking, because the neighbouring ruling forbids the single-number netting
  the marks-comparison needs. *Yours:* The rule is yours, and which of the two things
  "honest" was meant to test is a reading only its author can give.
- *If undecided:* Two beats keep applying two different tests, and every future board's
  quick-path check is graded by whichever one its author happened to read.

**Question.** Pick one: what does *"honest"* test?

**What the document says.** `docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:649-652`, under §7.2 (**RULED**,
`:636`): *"**A quick path must exist and must be honest.** If the board's short route is a
worse deal than the long one, the player did not opt in; they were priced in. The floor
send being free and always lit is the shape this already takes on the dispatch board."*

**What source says.** `docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:571-577`: free-and-always-lit is confirmed
by driving, *"but net marks per send put the free halt behind Mosswake at every roster (0W
6.800 vs 6.960 … 4W 8.240 vs 8.976), while `cfd-203:1270` applies §7.2's test **as EV** and
passes the line board on it."* And: *"(§7.3 four lines later forbids the single-number
netting an EV-only test performs.)"* — §7.3 is at `:671`.

**Blocked.** Any future board's §7.2 self-check; two beats currently apply two different
tests.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Free, always available, never locking* — because §7.3, four
lines later, forbids the single-number netting the EV reading requires, so the EV reading
makes the two ruled sections contradict. But §7.2 is yours and this is a rule
interpretation.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** Every board's quick-path check is graded by whichever test
its author happened to read.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** RULED BY COUNSEL ON DAVID'S
INSTRUCTION, 2026-09-09 — option (a). §7.2's 'honest' tests ACCESS, in three prongs: the
short route costs nothing to take, is always lit (no reachable state in which it goes
dark), and never locks the player out of the long one. It does not test payoff. A board's
§7.2 self-check is graded by driving those three prongs, not by comparing marks. cfd-203's
EV tables stay in the record as measurements and are annotated as surplus to the §7.2 test,
not as it. *(majority; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one moderated
debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in this block
are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named under Carried out moved some
of them.)*

- *Why:* The first reason is canon's own gloss on its own bullet, and at revision it leads,
  because the ruling had been resting its whole weight on a contradiction argument when it
  has a direct one. §7.2's quick-path bullet closes: 'The floor send being free and always
  lit is the shape this already takes on the dispatch board'
  (docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:592-594). Canon names its own honest quick path in two of
  the three prongs — free, and always lit — in the same breath as the sentence the entry
  reads as payoff language. The second reason is that the council may choose between readings
  of a RULED section but may not choose the reading that makes two RULED sections contradict:
  §7.3 (:604, RULED 2026-08-28, the later text) says any two options differing in BOTH
  currencies cannot be ranked, 'a comparison the board has deliberately given up the ability
  to make'. On the join board the two routes differ in both currencies, so the marks-EV
  ranking that reading (b) needs is exactly the netting §7.3 forbids. Third, and this kills
  (b) on its own terms: the EV reading has no determinate instrument. The same board, the
  same bytes, two in-repo measurements, opposite winners — cfd-203:1238 records 'Measured:
  the quick path is the better deal to level 3' from a marks-per-turn table, and
  sweep-2026-09-02.md:555-561 puts the free Halt behind Mosswake at every roster on net marks
  per send. 'Honest' as EV silently picks a divisor, which is the very failure the entry
  names. Prong (a) is drivable with no divisor to choose, is already the form the tree
  records it in (cfd-203:876 'The free halt, always lit, no reachable state dark';
  cfd-196:255), and re-grades nothing: the passed line board passes it today.
- *Dissent:* The skeptic's, that §7.2's two sentences carry both readings and reading the
  first out ('a worse deal … priced in' is payoff language) discards words in a RULED
  section, so the question belongs to David. It is right that this is a reading and not a
  finding, and its interim was the same as this ruling. It loses because the alternative
  reading requires a contradiction with the later RULED §7.3 and an arithmetic the repository
  has already performed twice with opposite signs — and because the access reading does not
  discard 'priced in': a player is priced in when the quick loop costs something to take or
  is not there to take, which is what the three prongs measure.
- *For David, not blocking:* None — choosing between readings of a RULED section is within
  the council's remit. Recorded for him in one line: if 'honest' was meant as
  marks-competitive, §7.3 needs a carve-out only he can write, and on the sweep's instrument
  the free Halt send fails it at every roster today.
- *Action:* [docs] Add a dated gloss under §7.2's quick-path bullet stating the three
  prongs — costs nothing to take, always lit with no reachable state dark, never locks the
  long route — marked as the council's reading on David's instruction, 2026-09-09, citing the
  bullet's own closing sentence at :592-594 as canon's gloss on two of the three and §7.3 as
  the reason the payoff reading is not available. Do not alter the bullet's own sentences.
  (C:/dev/skyrail-boards/docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md) · [docs] Supersede cfd-203:205-212
  ('it passes in the strongest direction — the short route is the better deal') and annotate
  the row at :1238 to say the §7.2 test is the access test and the board passes it by
  driving; the marks-per-turn table stays as a measurement and is not the test. Record beside
  it that the marks-per-send table in sweep-2026-09-02.md:555-561 reaches the opposite sign,
  which is why a single-number test was refused. Stated at revision, because it reads
  inconsistently with ruling 19 otherwise: this DOES supersede a sentence inside a passed
  board's signed beat, and ruling 19 declines to touch one, and the distinction is that here
  a claim was graded by the wrong instrument and is an error to correct in place under §7.7,
  while at 19 both readers measured beat and board consistent and there is no error to
  correct. Neither ruling edits a passed board's shipped bytes.
  (C:/dev/skyrail-boards/docs/cfd-203-beat.md) · [design] Carry the three prongs into the
  next beat's §7.2 self-check as a driven check, and add to FUTURE BOARDS' pre-registration
  lists the outcome 'nobody took the quick path' — the residual the access test cannot see.
  Stated in terms at revision: §7.5 is RULED and its own text is NOT edited by this ruling;
  the addition is to the pre-registration lists that future beats write under it.
- *Carried out:* at `1cf0bba` — `docs/cfd-203-beat.md` (the §7.2 honesty-test note: access,

## 16. The CFD-176 beat's Sees and Does describe a board that does not exist — four sentences

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: pick one of two or three.)*
For each of four sentences in the heat board's beat — "A stays held", "After return, wait",
"The outward step is what could die", and "One dispatch" — do you rewrite the sentence to
match the board as it plays, or accept it as your own shorthand and leave it?

- *Why:* Driving the shipped board shows each sentence is off: A is thin, not held, in two
  of the six states the board can reach, and both of those sit on the only path to WARM (the
  beat itself keeps thinning on and calls it visible, so it is on purpose); there is no
  control that waits — the wait fires from a timer after a pause; the step is set to "gone"
  every time without exception, so it can never survive; and reaching WARM takes four
  out-and-back trips, not one. The board passed and stands as played, so this is about
  whether its record tells the truth.
- *Options:*
  a. **Rewrite as measured** — Each sentence gets a dated correction stating what the board
     actually does; the beat then matches the board you sat. *Costs:* The record admits that a
     stake the beat promised — the step that could die — was never modelled. When the entry was
     written no test read this beat; since then a guard that checks beat sentences for words
     borrowed from the other lineage has started reading it, so a correction has to be run
     through the suite rather than assumed to leave it green.
  b. **Accept as shorthand** — The sentences stay, read as compressed authorial phrasing that
     the surrounding sentences are enough to decode. *Costs:* The beat keeps saying a player
     sees a held slab and a step that can die, when neither is so.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator would rewrite "A stays held"
  and "The outward step is what could die", which are plainly false about what a player sees
  and what can die, and leave "After return, wait" and "One dispatch" as shorthand
  recoverable from their neighbours. *Yours:* Whether your own compressed phrasing counts as
  shorthand or as a wrong claim is an author's call; one ruling shape can settle all four.
- *If undecided:* The record of a passed board goes on describing a stake it never carried
  — and this is the only passed board with no kill-ledger line and none of your own sit words
  in this repository, so here the beat is all there is.

**Question.** For each of four sentences: supersede as measured, or accept as authorial
ellipsis? One ruling shape covers all four.

**What the document says and what source says**, all re-derived at HEAD from
`C:\tmp\sweep176\results.json` and the shipped board:

| beat | the sentence | measured |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `cfd-176:23` | *"A stays held."* — the first sentence of Sees | `heldA` is **false in 2 of the 6 reachable states**, and both sit on the only path to WARM; `index.html:201` renders `destA` as `thin` there. The beat's own `:37` keeps held/thin ON and calls it *"visible as a held slab"*, so the thinning is intended and on screen. |
| `cfd-176:19` | *"After return, wait."* — stated as an imperative to the player | The player has **no control that waits**. `index.html:218-220` fires `board.wait()` from a 1500 ms idle timer inside `paint()`; none of the six tap bindings at `:253-273` reaches it. **This board is the origin of the timer convicted at audit §1.3.** |
| `cfd-176:27` | *"The outward step is what could die."* | `sim.js:74` sets `step = "gone"` unconditionally inside `wait()`. Replayed over every reachable banked state: **step can never remain `out`.** The stake was asserted, not modelled. |
| `cfd-176:19` | *"One dispatch."* | The path to WARM is **four** mandatory out-and-back trips; exactly 1 of 24 orderings reaches `banked`. |

**Blocked.** Nothing — `/dawnspur-heat/` is passed and stands as played, and **no test
reads this beat** (`grep -rl 'cfd-176-beat' test/` returns zero files), so every
supersession here is free of a regex re-grade.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Supersede `:23` and `:27`; leave `:19`'s two as ellipsis.* The
first two are measurably false about what a player sees and what can die — the third row
in particular is a stake the beat promised and the board never modelled. The two `:19`
readings are recoverable from the surrounding sentences and are the ellipsis class the
audit already routed to you at its `:1258`.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** The record of a passed board describes a stake it never
carried — and it is the only board whose pass has no `KILLS.md` line and no verbatim in
this repository *(as of 2026-09-02; a `KILLS.md` record was entered 2026-09-09 under counsel's ruling on entry 7)*.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** RULED BY COUNSEL ON DAVID'S
INSTRUCTION, 2026-09-09 — per sentence, not one shape for all four, and REVISED at check:
no sentence in this beat is rewritten. cfd-176:27 'The outward step is what could die' —
option (a), superseded as measured, with the sentence KEPT VERBATIM and a dated measured
note appended after it. This is expressly NOT cfd-176:11's rewrite-plus-parenthetical form,
which is the opposite of what is intended here: at :11 the sentence was restated as current
fact and the earlier words preserved in a parenthetical ('An earlier cut read "Superheavy
beat only. No implement. No deploy. No merge."; true on the day of signature, record
now.'), and cfd-183:45 and :182 do the same. Here David's sentence stands and the note goes
beside it. cfd-176:23 'A stays held' — option (b), accept, with a dated scoping note
appended. cfd-176:19 'After return, wait' and 'One dispatch' — option (b), accept as
authorial ellipsis, no note. The entry's claim that no test reads this beat is stale and is
corrected in the same edit. *(split; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic —
one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in
this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named under Carried out
moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* :27 is unanimous across all four counsellors and the moderator confirmed the
  mechanism at source — wait() reads 'if (s.step === "out") s.step = "gone"' with no
  condition (sit/dawnspur-heat/sim.js:74, inside wait() at :72-79), fired by the 1500 ms idle
  timer at sit/dawnspur-heat/index.html:218-220, and the beat's own row at :34 promised the

  line and no sit verbatim in the repository (cfd-176:11), so the beat is the whole record of

  held. The stone keeps a banked mass … A step sits on ground off A. On the home wait the
  hearth does not dim. The step can go. No leftover GOODS lit.' — every clause is post-WARM,

  thinning is on purpose. The distinction between the three sentences is NOT 'whether David's

- *For David, not blocking:* None, but this is the ruling in this set most likely to be
  flipped by one word from him: if Sees was meant to describe the whole path rather than the
  banked scene, :23 is a wrong claim and takes a fuller correction. The note is written so
  that flip costs one edit.

## 17. `cfd-206:60` cites World Bible §12 as canon, and a test pins the citation

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a yes/no.)*
The Mosswake Loop beat tells readers to cite World Bible §12 as canon; that bible was ruled
legacy 33 minutes after the beat merged, and a test fails if the citation is removed — do
you append a dated note saying §12 is retired, or leave the line as it is?

- *Why:* Two later beats carry the opposite instruction, "Do not cite World Bible §12", and
  each of those is pinned by its own test, so the three beats now disagree about what is
  canon. Deleting the citation from the Mosswake beat turns its test red; adding a note after
  it keeps all three tests green.
- *Options:*
  a. **Append a dated note** — The citation stays in place with a bracketed, dated line after
     it recording that §12 was ruled legacy and is superseded. *Costs:* A docs edit and nothing
     else; the three tests stay green.
  b. **Leave it** — The beat keeps instructing readers to cite §12 as canon. *Costs:* A
     reader of that beat is sent to a bible you retired, and it contradicts the two later beats.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator would append the note — it is
  the only edit that fixes the reader's problem without touching a test. *Yours:* The entry
  names nothing as yours beyond the choice itself: whether a beat's canon list may carry a
  dated correction after the fact, or stays exactly as it was when the beat merged, is a
  convention only you set.
- *If undecided:* Either a beat keeps citing a superseded bible as canon, or someone
  deletes the line and the test suite goes red for a documentation edit.

**Question.** Pick one: append a dated parenthetical, or leave it?

**What the document says.** *(Quoted before the 2026-09-09 note was appended to that line under counsel's ruling on this entry; the line number holds, the quoted run no longer does.)* `docs/cfd-206-beat.md:60`: *"World Bible §12 Mosswake Loop. Core
Loop: nodes and lines. Geology:"*.

**What source says.** Measured at HEAD: `test/mosswake-loop.test.js:226` asserts
`assert.match(beat, /World Bible §12/)` against `docs/cfd-206-beat.md` — **so removing the
citation goes red.** Two later beats carry the opposite instruction, also pinned:
`test/herbs-larder.test.js:265` and `test/they-remember.test.js:300` both assert
`/Do not cite World Bible §12/`. The audit records the §12 source was ruled LEGACY 33
minutes after the merge (`docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:1273-1274`).

**Blocked.** Nothing. The parenthetical form keeps all three regexes green; a removal does
not.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Append the parenthetical.* It is the only edit that fixes the
reader's problem without touching a test, and the measurement above is why.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** A beat keeps citing a superseded bible as canon, or the
suite goes red for a docs edit.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** RULED BY COUNSEL ON DAVID'S
INSTRUCTION, 2026-09-09 — option (a). Append a dated bracketed note after
docs/cfd-206-beat.md:60 recording that World Bible v0.1 is LEGACY except §8 and that §12 is
not to be cited as live canon, attributed to the two later beats that say so in their own
words. The signed line itself is kept verbatim. *(unanimous; four lenses — canon, cost, the
reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not
David's words. Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the
landing named under Carried out moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* Unanimous, and the measurement is why. Verified at HEAD by the moderator, and
  re-verified at revision: test/mosswake-loop.test.js:226 asserts match(beat, /World Bible
  §12/) against docs/cfd-206-beat.md, so a deletion goes red; test/herbs-larder.test.js:265
  and test/they-remember.test.js:300 assert /Do not cite World Bible §12/ against their own
  beats; no doesNotMatch anywhere in test/ takes a beat as its first argument. The appended
  note is the only edit that fixes the reader's problem while leaving all three regexes
  green. The retirement is not Linear-only — it is in the tree, in two later beats' own
  words: cfd-207:86-87 'World Bible v0.1 is LEGACY except §8 (sections 2, 4, 6). Do not cite
  World Bible §12 as live canon.' and cfd-208:106. The line at :60 sits inside the 'Canon
  this sitting' list under the signed-beat heading, so it is annotated and not rewritten.
  §7.7 names the beat as the record superseded in place, so the convention question the entry
  routes to David is already answered by his own later RULED text.
- *Dissent:* None on the choice. One contested detail, settled: the audit's '33 minutes
  after the merge' is the audit's figure and this repository cannot time it, so the note
  cites cfd-207:86-87 and cfd-208:106 as the in-repo authority and does not carry the 33
  minutes into the beat as a measured fact. Prefer null to a plausible number.
- *For David, not blocking:* None.
- *Action:* [docs] Append a dated bracketed note after docs/cfd-206-beat.md:60, keeping the
  signed line verbatim: World Bible v0.1 was subsequently ruled LEGACY except §8, and §12 is
  not to be cited as live canon — quoting cfd-207:86-87 and citing cfd-208:106, dated,

## 18. Canon `:826`'s confabulated mechanism — which question produced it?

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: pick one of two or three.)*
Canon quotes one player answer — "I noticed it degrade and started to see a connection
between fully tended land and success rates" — as a made-up mechanism and the reason to
refuse asking players what they intended; did that answer come from the TEND question or
from the trim question?

- *Why:* The sit that produced the answer is recorded only in Linear, not in this
  repository. The closest thing on file, from the storm board's second sit, is a different
  sentence with the same content, and it too is you explaining why you tended, while the trim
  question got a plain factual answer. If the TEND question produced the quoted answer, then
  the very question shape canon holds up as the one that works is the one that produced the
  answer canon cites against intent questions — and the later pre-registration ruling rests
  on that reading.
- *Options:*
  a. **The TEND question** — Canon's blessed question produced its own counter-example; the
     argument in that section rests on the very question it praises, and so does the
     pre-registration ruling built on it. *Costs:* Two canon sections rest on that answer — the
     one that quotes it and the pre-registration ruling built on it — and the uncomfortable fact
     goes into the record.
  b. **The trim question** — The refused question produced the made-up answer, as canon
     currently implies; the sections stand as written. *Costs:* None to the text, but it has to
     be true — the repo's own trace points the other way.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator gives no read: this is a lookup
  of what actually happened in a sit the repository does not hold, and the in-repo trace
  pointing at TEND is not a substitute for that record. *Yours:* Only you sat that board and
  only Linear holds the transcript, so which question you were answering is yours to state.
- *If undecided:* Canon's central case for refusing intent questions may be built on an
  answer produced by the question it blesses, and nobody can say either way.

**Question.** Pick one: did the **TEND** question or the **trim** question produce the
confabulated answer §7.4 cites?

**What the document says.** `docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:825-828`: *"The one intent
answer on record is a **confabulated mechanism** — 'I noticed it degrade and started to see
a connection between fully tended land and success rates' — a link that does not exist on
that board."*

**What source says.** `docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:585-591`: Linear-only; *"the closest in-repo
record (`sit/dawnspur-storm/sim.js:52-53`, second sit) is a **different** sentence with the
same content, and it too is an answer about **why he tended** — while the intent trim
question got a clean factual answer (`sim.js:49-50`, 'TRIM landed (considered at each storm
send and left)')."* And the stake: *"If the TEND question did, then the shape §7.4 calls
'the one that DOES work' produced the one confabulated answer it cites as the reason to
refuse intent questions — and §7.5 (RULED) rests on that reading at `:681-686`."* That
§7.5 passage is `docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:915` at HEAD — *"**§7.4 got here first and
filed it as a detail.** It cites the TEND question as"* — the sweep's `:681-686` is stale.

**Blocked.** Nothing mechanically, but two ruled sections rest on the answer.

**Orchestrator's read.** *No read — this is a Linear lookup, not a judgement.* The repo
evidence points at the TEND question and that is the uncomfortable answer, but the
repository does not hold the sit and I will not guess it.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** §7.4's central argument for refusing intent questions is
built on an answer produced by the question it blesses.

**RULED IN PART — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09; the rest RETURNED TO
DAVID.** REQUIRES DAVID, unanimously — the council refuses to rule this one. Neither option
may be chosen, because both assert a fact about a sit that this repository does not hold
and that no command available to the council can measure. Interim: canon is not edited; the
provenance is recorded as unverified in this ledger entry, together with the nearest
in-repo trace, marked as a trace and not the record. *(unanimous; four lenses — canon,
cost, the reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision.
Not David's words. Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read;
the landing named under Carried out moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* Every counsellor independently reached the same refusal, and it is the ambient
  contract's first rule applied to a lookup: prefer null to a plausible number, and an
  unavailable measurement is reported as unavailable. The sit is Linear-only; the
  linear-server connector is unauthenticated in this session, so the transcript is
  unavailable, not absent. What the repository does hold is a trace, and it points at TEND
  without being the record: sit/dawnspur-storm/sim.js:49-53, David's own second-sit recut
  note, records the trim question getting a plain factual answer ('TRIM landed (considered at
  each storm send and left)') and, in the same breath, 'TEND was still welded to the percent
  — "I tended particularly when the storms came in to see if it would improve my success
  chances"' — a percent theory of the same shape as the sentence canon quotes, but a
  different sentence. Added at revision, and it bears on how the answer is used: that trace
  is a PASSED board's frozen bytes and cannot be corrected in either direction, so whichever
  way David answers, nothing in sit/dawnspur-storm/ moves — his answer moves canon and this
  ledger only. Choosing (a) would write into canon that David's blessed question produced
  canon's own counter-example on the strength of a resemblance; choosing (b) would assert the
  comfortable answer on nothing. One correction to the entry's stake, checked at HEAD: §7.5

  in different shapes' (:827-832, this council's earlier cite of :826-829 corrected; find it
  by text) — and §7.4's heading reads WRITTEN DOWN, not RULED (:666; its status is entry 5's
  question, not this council's). So the answer corrects §7.4's prose either way and overturns
  no ruling. The entry's cite of the §7.5 passage as :681-686 is the sweep's stale number.
- *Dissent:* The canon lens would additionally place a dated provenance note at canon

  which is where the other three lenses put them. If David does not answer within a sweep or

- *Requires David:* The answer canon quotes at docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:751-754 — 'I
  noticed it degrade and started to see a connection between fully tended land and success

  will not guess it. Note that that trace is a passed board's frozen bytes and is not
  correctable either way, so your answer moves canon's prose and this ledger, nothing else.
- *Action:* [record] Record entry 18 in docs/decisions-open-2026-09-02.md as REQUIRES DAVID

## 19. `cfd-183` does not state the mark gate

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a yes/no.)*
The scale board's beat describes the Mosswake carry — pays +1, +2, +3 by level — but never
states the marks condition under which that send is allowed (on the heat board's rule, the
send lights only with a mark in hand), saying only that the gate is "carried from the
played board's cadence", which both people who checked it read as the heat board's rule; do
you want the gate written out in the beat, or does specifying it by reference stand?

- *Why:* Both people who checked it agree the beat and the board are consistent — the gate
  exists in the code and the beat's reference to it is unambiguous. The only question is
  whether a beat may specify a rule by reference rather than in words.
- *Options:*
  a. **State it explicitly** — The beat gains a sentence spelling out when the Mosswake carry
     may be sent. *Costs:* An edit to a passed board's beat for a rule that is already
     unambiguous by reference.
  b. **Leave the reference** — "Gate carried from the played board's cadence" stands as the
     specification. *Costs:* A future reader has to open the heat board's code to learn the
     gate.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator would leave it — both readers
  measured the beat and board as consistent, and the reference points at exactly one place.
  *Yours:* Whether beats in this project may specify by reference is a register rule only you
  set.
- *If undecided:* A future reader of the beat re-derives a gate that was already specified
  — nothing is blocked and the board passed.

**Question.** Yes or no: does the beat state the mark gate explicitly, or does
specification-by-reference stand?

**What the document says.** `docs/cfd-183-beat.md:59-62`, the CARRY FOOD bullet: *"the
profit leg, as played: pays marks by the level (+1, +2, +3; the ladder's +4 is never paid,
because topped at 4 is the stop and the carry goes dark with it …)"* — no mark gate stated.
`:238` states the gate only as *"gate carried from the played board's cadence"*.

**What source says.** `docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:1015-1016`, under *refuted at
review*: *"cfd-183 M4's 'stricter than signed' (the mark gate is specified by reference to
heat `sim.js:22`)"*, and `:1021-1022` puts it in do-not-average — *"sweeper DAVID, refuter
NONE; both agree the beat and the board are consistent."*

**Blocked.** Nothing. The board passed; both readers agree it is consistent.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Leave it.* Both readers measured the beat and the board as
consistent; the only question is register, and the reference is unambiguous.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** A future reader re-derives a gate that was already specified.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** RULED BY COUNSEL ON DAVID'S
INSTRUCTION, 2026-09-09 — option (b). Specification stands; docs/cfd-183-beat.md is not
edited on this account. The beat states the Mosswake carry's gate in words twice — :67-68
'costs 1, pays 3, off-board ground. Lit only while the terrace is below full, armed once
per carry' and :137 'costs 1, pays 3; lit below full, armed once per carry' — and the board
enforces exactly that. Two findings are recorded rather than built. *(majority; four lenses
— canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one
revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the
council read; the landing named under Carried out moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* The board passed, both people who checked measured beat and board consistent, and
  the audit put it in do-not-average as 'sweeper DAVID, refuter NONE'. The entry's premise
  that the beat 'never states the marks condition' is narrower than the beat: the moderator
  opened it and :67-68 and :137 both state the lit condition in words, and 'costs 1' carries
  the mark requirement — on this board canB is '!topped() && s.reserve < RESERVE_FULL &&
  s.marks >= 1 && s.bArmed' (sit/dawnspur-scale/sim.js:49-51), so the mark clause is exactly
  the cost. Editing a passed board's beat to restate a rule the beat already states and a
  test already grades is a change with no error to correct, and this project's record shows
  that class of change introducing its own. (The contrast with ruling 15, which does
  supersede a sentence in a passed board's signed beat: there a claim was graded by the wrong
  instrument and is an error; here there is no error.)
- *Dissent:* The reader's, and it is not defeated so much as re-routed. Its finding is real
  and the moderator confirmed it at source: the two neighbouring bullets have the same
  sentence shape and differ by one clause — TEND at :65 is 'Lit only while the terrace is
  below full and a mark is in hand', MOSSWAKE at :67-68 is 'Lit only while the terrace is
  below full, armed once per carry' — and parallel bullets that differ by a clause read as a
  contrast, so the natural inference is that MOSSWAKE needs no mark, which is false. It loses
  because 'costs 1' is in the same sentence and both readers who measured it derived the gate
  correctly, so the hazard is a reading cost on a passed record rather than a defect. Its
  named remedy — re-pointing the :238 reference at sit/dawnspur-scale/sim.js:51 instead of
  'the played board's cadence' — is declined for the same reason as the rest, and this is
  stated at revision rather than left silent: it is the same class of edit to a passed
  board's signed beat that this ruling refuses, so accepting it would be inconsistent with
  the ruling that contains it. The skeptic added the second finding, also confirmed: the :238
  reference, 'gate carried from the played board's cadence', resolves to heat's canB
  (sit/dawnspur-heat/sim.js:20-22: phase 'b', marks >= 1, bUsed === 0) and does not yield the
  scale board's reserve clause — so the reference locates the ancestor cadence, not the gate
  as this board enforces it, and the entry's 'the reference points at exactly one place,
  unambiguous' is stronger than the record.
- *For David, not blocking:* None. Whether beats may specify by reference is a register
  rule he sets; the ruling does not set it, it declines to change a passed beat where both
  readers found beat and board consistent.
- *Action:* [record] Mark entry 19 RULED (b, no change) in
  docs/decisions-open-2026-09-02.md and record the two dissent findings so they are not
  re-found: (i) cfd-183:65 and :67-68 are parallel bullets differing by the mark clause,
  which reads as a contrast even though 'costs 1' carries the requirement; (ii) the :238
  reference resolves to heat sim.js:20-22 and under-specifies the scale board's own gate,
  whose reserve clause is stated at :67-68 and enforced at sit/dawnspur-scale/sim.js:49-51.
  Record also that the reader's proposed remedy — re-pointing :238 at the scale sim — was
  declined as the same class of edit this ruling refuses, so the option is in the record and
  not lost. (C:/dev/skyrail-boards/docs/decisions-open-2026-09-02.md) · [design] The next
  beat that inherits the Mosswake carry states the gate in its own words — below full, a mark
  in hand, armed once per carry — rather than by reference to cfd-183, because the reference
  resolves to the ancestor's cadence and not to the enforced gate. That is where the words
  are being written anyway.
- *Carried out:* this record — RULED (b, no change); the gate is restated by the next beat
  that inherits the Mosswake carry, in its own words, when that beat is cut.
