## 42. `/dawnspur-line/` speaks "stake undefined" to screen-reader players — fix a passed, pinned board, or record it?

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a design call.)*
The line board passed and its bytes are frozen by a pin, but every sendable card that is
not already out has a spoken label reading the literal word "undefined" where the stake
should be — three of four cards at the opening, two of four with a send out (a card whose
send is away says "No Wardens ride with nothing staked" instead) — so do you fix the one
line and re-pin the board, or leave the passed bytes alone and record the defect?

- *Why:* The label is built from a field the line board's cards do not have — their stake
  is food, and the visible card uses a different field for it — so screen-reader players hear
  "undefined" while sighted players see the right text. Fixing it means editing a passed
  board and re-hashing its hub card and everything under it, and you have not yet made a rule
  for when a passed board's bytes may be moved.
- *Options:*
  a. **Fix it and re-pin** — One line in the board's page is corrected, and the hub card and
     every descendant pin are re-hashed and recut. *Costs:* The first re-pin of a passed board,
     done before any written rule says when re-pins are allowed; nothing about a pin is cheap —
     every hash under the hub card moves.
  b. **Leave the bytes, record the defect** — The passed board is untouched; the defect goes
     into the record as known and shipped. *Costs:* A passed board says "undefined" to every
     screen-reader player, indefinitely, on the one lineage whose whole economy is the stake.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator would fix it and treat the
  re-pin as the price of the fix, since a label no sighted sit ever saw is not evidence any
  pass rested on. *Yours:* Whether spoken accessibility text is inside or outside what a pass
  froze is a rule you have not yet made, and it is yours to make.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked — the board is live and passed — but every
  screen-reader player keeps hearing "undefined" on the sendable cards, and the project still
  has no rule for when a pinned board's bytes may be touched.

*(Added 2026-09-08 from the DOM namespace sweep, call 1 — `docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:1901`;
finding 10 at `:1501`. Numbered by arrival, placed by tier: it publishes something measurably
wrong to a reader.)*

**Question.** `/dawnspur-line/` passed and is hash-pinned (hub card and descendants). Every
sendable card's `aria-label` on it that is not *out* reads the literal word `undefined` where
the stake should be — three of four cards at the opening, two of four with a send in the air;
the *out* branch of the template says "No Wardens ride with nothing staked" instead
(`index.html:413-420`, driven at review). Is a one-line fix to a passed board's `index.html` —
re-hashing and recutting the pins — owed, or are the passed bytes left alone and the defect
recorded?

**What the document says.** The record, `docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:1901-1906`: `index.html:419`
reads `c.stake`; line's cards carry no `stake` key — its stake is food and the visible card
uses `stakeText` — so the label is built from `undefined`. Reproduced by the synthesis and by
the sweep's wiring pair. The ledger's standing rule is that a guard never forces a board edit;
the record says in the same breath that this is not a guard, it is a shipped accessibility
defect.

**What source says.** `sit/dawnspur-line/index.html:419` (the `aria-label` template) and
`sit/dawnspur-line/sim.js` (no `stake:` on a line card; `stakeText` is the rendered form).

**Blocked.** Nothing. The board is live and passed; the defect reaches only players using a
screen reader, and reaches all of them.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Fix it, and treat the re-pin as the price of the fix rather than a
reason not to.* A pinned board is pinned so a sit's evidence is not rewritten under it; a
label no sighted sit ever saw is not evidence any sit rested on. But the pin census
(`boards-pin-census`) says no board's bytes are "near-free" to move — the hub card and every
descendant re-hash — and whether a11y text is inside or outside what a pass froze is a rule
you have not made yet. Yours.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** Left: a passed board says "undefined" to every screen-reader
player, indefinitely, on the one lineage whose economy is the stake. Fixed carelessly: the
first re-pin of a passed board without a written rule for when re-pins are allowed.

**RULED IN PART — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09; the rest RETURNED TO
DAVID.** RULED BY COUNSEL ON DAVID'S INSTRUCTION, 2026-09-09: option (b) — leave the passed
bytes of /dawnspur-line/ untouched and record the defect; the rule question goes back to
David. REVISED after an adversarial check; the choice, the holding and the refusal of
option (a) are unchanged and re-verified, four measured figures in the pin census were
wrong and are replaced, one quotation was attributed to boards that do not carry it, and
the guard this ruling had refused is adopted. THE HOLDING. The spoken label at
sit/dawnspur-line/index.html:419 (and its byte-identical copy under public/) keeps saying
"stake undefined" until David rules on whether a pass freezes spoken text. The council
refuses to open the pin and refuses to pretend that (b) discharges the defect. (1) THE
EXACT PATCH, which is NOT the one the entry assumes. The parity form is c.name + ", pays "
+ c.pays + ", " + (c.condition === null ? stakeText(c) : c.condition) + ", " + c.percent +
" percent home". Line's visible face at index.html:404 reads c.condition === null ?
stakeText(c) : c.condition; stakeText at index.html:347-351 returns only "free", "N from
the stores" or "N from the stores · toll T" and never the condition; conditionFor at
sim.js:337-346 is non-null exactly when a sendable card cannot send, so the wrong aria
branch (index.html:417-419, the sendable-and-not-out branch) is the one covering unlit
cards, where the sighted player reads "The stores hold 0. Mosswake wants 2." (sim.js:342).
Storm's bare stakeText(c) — the form the entry and three counsellors treated as the fix —
restores the stake and still speaks the wrong thing on a conditioned card. Option (a) as
written is half a fix. (2) THE PIN CENSUS, MEASURED AT e226a9f AND CORRECTED. sha256
b6f21db0dd8a2d4ee9373f859859748c5c0778204a3cad6ee95466c338d43f2a, identical in sit/ and
public/, is pinned in: the hub card, public/index.html:87 (short form); 16 full-sha lines
across 8 descendant test files (dawnspur-halt:85-86, dawnspur-site:88-89,
dice-at-the-places:149-150, herbs-larder:106-107, mosswake-loop:93-94,
still-standing:335-336, they-remember:125-126, two-ways-from-here:243-244); 6 short-sha
list entries in 6 of those 8 (dice, herbs, mosswake, still-standing, they-remember,
two-ways — halt and site carry full shas only); sit+public dawnspur-line/MANIFEST.txt:102,
2 lines; 18 lineage lines across 9 OTHER boards' sit+public MANIFESTs (halt, site, storm,
dice, herbs, mosswake, still-standing, they-remember, two-ways, in sit/ and public/ each);
and 10 lines across 9 David-signed beat documents (cfd-201:99, 205:40, 205-halt:43, 206:45,
207:57, 208:66 and :508, 209:104, 210:238, 212-still-standing:292). 53 lines in all, plus
one non-beat hit in docs/decisions-open-2026-09-02.md:1963. FOUR FIGURES IN THE FIRST
CONSENSUS WERE WRONG and are replaced here: it said 20 lineage lines across 10 other boards
(measured 18 across 9 — the 20/10 count folds in the line board's own two MANIFEST lines,
already counted separately), 10 signed beat DOCUMENTS (measured 9 documents, 10 lines —
cfd-208 carries two), FIVE of the eight descendant tests belonging to passed boards
(measured SEVEN — dawnspur-site is the only one that is not, public/index.html:73 "live —
sat 2026-08-30, not passed", every other of the eight reads "passed its sit"), and one of
SIXTEEN hub cards (measured 15). All eight descendant tests — not five — assert the lock by
name, with the message "moved — the lineage lock is the one rule that never bends"
(halt:101, site:104, dice:169 and :171, herbs:126 and :128, mosswake:113 and :115,
still-standing:357 and :359, they-remember:145 and :147, two-ways:263 and :265). The
corrections cut the census in two places and widen it in two; the direction of the argument
is unchanged and its strongest fact was understated, not overstated. (3) THE KILL-LINE
ATTRIBUTION, CORRECTED. A re-pin reddens the lineage-lock assertion in all eight descendant
tests, seven of them belonging to passed boards. Those boards' signed beats forbid it IN
THEIR OWN WORDS: "Overwrite any live board." (docs/cfd-205-beat.md:246,
docs/cfd-205-halt-beat.md:269), "Overwrite any live pin." (cfd-206:213, cfd-207:241,
cfd-208:278), and "/they-remember/ is a PASSED board and does not get overwritten."
(cfd-208:321), the same for /dice-at-the-places/ (cfd-209:22). The verbatim "Any existing
board's bytes are touched" appears in exactly two documents — docs/cfd-203-beat.md:1020 and
docs/cfd-201-beat.md:1002, the line board's own beat and its parent's. The first consensus
put that sentence in the descendants' mouths. The substance survives — a re-pin edits
guards that passes rested on — but David is not to be handed a quotation over boards that
do not carry it. (4) A FINDING MEASURED DURING THIS DEBATE. /dawnspur-storm/ (live, "sat
five times, stopped without a pass", public/index.html:79) carries the lesser half of the
same divergence: its visible face at sit/dawnspur-storm/index.html:469-470 is
condition-or-stakeText, its aria at :497-498 is bare stakeText(c). Not the council's to
fix; it belongs to the beat that owns storm's lineage. (5) THE GUARD IS ADOPTED, and the
first consensus's refusal of it is withdrawn as a strawman. That refusal answered a general
rule (an aria template may not read a key its sim's cards() does not produce), which would
be RED on passed bytes today and is therefore entry 43's forward-scoping question. It is
not what the canon lens proposed. The proposal was a test GREEN at HEAD on the passed bytes
— asserting that :419 still reads c.stake and that the sim's cards() carry no stake key —
which reddens the moment a fix lands and prints the re-pin list. That is not entry 43's
question: entry 43 asks whether a rule may be scoped to boards passing after today,
precisely because its plain form "would be red on three passed boards today and could only
go green by editing them" (docs/decisions-open-2026-09-02.md, entry 43). This guard is
green on them and reddens only on the FIX, which is the ratchet direction the ambient
contract mandates: C:/dev/skyrail/CLAUDE.md:235-240, "Self-cancelling guards over comments.
When you must leave a known defect in place, pin it with a test that goes red the moment it
is fixed and prints the cleanup list ... Verify the guard red-first, or it is decoration",
and :242, "A guard must fire at the person who can act on it." The surviving objection —
that the 16 sha pins already detect the byte move — is true about detection and misses what
the contract asks a guard for: what those pins print is "moved — the lineage lock is the
one rule that never bends", an alarm against the sanctioned fix rather than a cleanup list
for it, and they name none of the other 37 pin sites. The guard is built in the boards repo
at test/dawnspur-line.test.js, which is not a passed board's byte (the board ships three
files — MANIFEST.txt, index.html, sim.js) and is sha-pinned nowhere. (6) FORWARD ACTION
WITHIN THE RULES. The next cut that forks this card template inherits the parity form above
as a kill-list item, so the defect stops with /dawnspur-line/ and no descendant carries it.
*(majority; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one
adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in this block are at
`e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named under Carried out moved some of
them.)*

- *Why:* Four counsellors, four independent routes, one destination: (b) as the
  in-authority ruling and the rule question back to David. Nothing in the record supports
  (a), and (a) is outside the council's charter besides. Every citation below was re-opened
  at e226a9f for this revision. (1) THE LOCK IS RULED AND UNSCOPED.
  docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:407-409, §7 under the heading "### What does NOT change"
  (:405): "**The lineage lock.** Never overwrite a passed or killed board. Every cumulative
  sitting ships at a new sibling path, and the boards it inherits from stay live and
  untouched at their own paths." It is written as bytes, not as evidence, and nothing in
  canon scopes it to what a sitter perceived. (2) THE EDIT IS A KILL, NOT A COST. The board's
  own signed beat, docs/cfd-203-beat.md:1019-1021: "Live shas are overwritten (576ce2b6,
  953368a1, 292d6645, 395c18f2, 5ad814e6). Any existing board's bytes are touched." Seven of
  the eight descendant test files that pin this sha belong to PASSED boards (measured;
  dawnspur-site is the only exception, public/index.html:73), and all eight assert the lock
  by name. Their own signed beats forbid the overwrite in their own words — "Overwrite any
  live board." (cfd-205:246, cfd-205-halt:269), "Overwrite any live pin." (cfd-206:213,
  cfd-207:241, cfd-208:278), "is a PASSED board and does not get overwritten" (cfd-208:321,
  cfd-209:22). So a re-pin does not merely move hashes: it edits the guard a pass rested on,
  for seven passed boards, to make a kill line's own test accept the thing it kills. That is
  the ambient contract's forbidden move — "Move ceiling down as work lands. Never move it up
  to make a run pass", C:/dev/skyrail/CLAUDE.md. (3) THE CLOSEST RULED ANALOGUE IS FIVE DAYS
  OLD AND POINTS AWAY FROM THE ORCHESTRATOR'S READ. §7.7 (RULED, David, 2026-09-04),
  docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:1029: "A frozen artifact records BYTES, never the state of
  the world around them." Its worked instance is a known-false sentence inside
  sit/still-standing/MANIFEST.txt — :1048-1049, "the file is inside the pinned directory, so
  neither can ever be corrected. A passed board is not recut, and that rule is exactly why
  the file is trustworthy about its bytes." David accepted permanence of a falsehood in a
  passed artifact rather than open a pin. Carving spoken text out of the pin is a new rule of
  the same family, and the council may not make one. (4) THE ORCHESTRATOR'S PREMISE IS
  UNMEASURED. "A label no sighted sit ever saw is not evidence any sit rested on" rests on a
  modality nobody recorded: the verdict is the single word "passes"
  (docs/cfd-203-beat.md:1549-1551) and no sit record states how the board was played. The
  contract prefers null to a plausible number. What IS measured supports only the read's
  weaker form — test/dawnspur-line.test.js contains zero aria-label assertions, and
  docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:1305 records "No test reads line's aria-label" — but a fact about
  what the tests grade is not a rule about what the pin freezes. (5) THE DEFECT IS REAL,
  RE-VERIFIED FROM SOURCE, AND CONFINED. sit/dawnspur-line/sim.js:352-372 builds cards with
  keys id name line sendable lit out pays provisions toll chance percent note condition
  provisional manifestLine — no stake — in both the sendable and the non-sendable branch,
  while index.html:418-419 interpolates ", stake " + c.stake, so the string is literally
  "stake undefined" at every frame on every sendable card that is not out. A grep of c.stake
  across sit/ and public/ index.html returns three source sites on two boards:
  dawnspur-dispatch:277 and :345, where dispatch's cards DO carry stake and the template is
  correct, and dawnspur-line:419, where they do not. (The first consensus named only
  dispatch:345 and line:419; dispatch:277 is a third site, on the board where the key exists,
  and the conclusion is unaffected.) Nothing downstream inherits it: storm already dropped
  the key reference. (6) THE PRICE. (b) costs one board's spoken stake, indefinitely, on one
  of FIFTEEN hub cards (measured). (a) costs the one rule canon lists as unbending, spent
  without a written rule, on the manoeuvre the pin was built to catch: protection commit
  7f58c8f measured that a coordinated overwrite of both sim.js copies, both MANIFESTs and the
  index card "passed 213/213 GREEN", and built the HEAD-blob pin as the only guard that
  survives it. The record is the honest surface here; the board is not. (7) ON CONFIDENCE.
  Unanimous on the CHOICE — all four counsellors reach (b) with the rule question returned.
  Not unanimous on the ACTIONS: canon asked for the self-cancelling test as something
  "dictated by the ambient contract, not chosen"; reader asked for it as item (3) alongside
  the kill-list item; cost called it "an implementation detail of how the record is made
  load-bearing, not a third option". The first consensus adopted the kill-list item and
  refused the guard, carrying a 3-1 minority position under a "unanimous" label. This
  revision adopts the guard, so the action set is now the majority ask, and the label is set
  to majority to keep the two halves distinguishable.
- *Dissent:* THE STRONGEST DISSENT is the cost/reversibility and reader lenses' shared one:
  (b) is right as an interim and wrong as a terminal state. A screen-reader player who opens
  /dawnspur-line/ today hears a false stake on the one lineage whose entire economy IS the
  stake, and recording a defect does not discharge it — it only keeps the record truthful.
  That argument is not refuted, it is deferred: it loses on authority alone. The council may
  not open a pin, and §7.7 shows David choosing permanence over a recut in a materially
  similar case five days ago, so the counter-argument is his to accept, not ours. Two answers
  narrow the damage without touching a byte: the defect stops at this board (nothing
  downstream reads c.stake), and the kill-list item makes the parity form mandatory on the
  next cut. THE SECOND DISSENT IS WITHDRAWN, and the withdrawal is recorded rather than
  deleted because it was the check's finding. The first consensus refused the canon lens's
  guard by answering a general form nobody proposed — a rule that an aria template may not
  read a key its sim's cards() does not produce, which would be red on three passed boards
  today and is therefore entry 43's scoping question. The form actually proposed is green at
  HEAD and reddens on the fix; entry 43 does not reach it. The one objection that survived
  the strawman — "it adds no detection", since 16 sha pins already redden when those bytes
  move — is true about detection and is not what the ambient contract asks a guard for
  (C:/dev/skyrail/CLAUDE.md:235-240). Refusing the guard might have been defensible; refusing
  it against a proposal nobody made was not. WHAT REMAINS CONTESTABLE IN THE ADOPTED GUARD,
  stated so the carrier does not mistake it for settled. It is a THIRD coupling of
  test/dawnspur-line.test.js to bytes it may not move, and a guard that pins a defect can
  calcify it — which is why it must print the parity patch and the full census, not merely
  fail. And its mutation check does not have the shape the adversarial check assumed
  ("confirming exactly this test reddens"): patching :419 in a scratch clone reddens more
  than one test. Derived from source, to be confirmed by the carrier and not by this council:
  the 16 descendant pins read HEAD, not disk (test/dawnspur-line.test.js:66-68, git cat-file
  blob HEAD:path), so an UNCOMMITTED patch leaves them green; test/boards-index.test.js:76
  reads a dirty path from disk, so the hub-card hash test reddens;
  test/dawnspur-line.test.js:212-218 grades the MANIFEST against disk, so it reddens; and the
  sit/public byte-identity test reddens if only one copy is patched. The carrier records the
  actual red set from the run. If the new guard is NOT among the reds, it is decoration and
  must not land.
- *Requires David:* Is spoken accessibility text — an aria-label and its kin — inside or
  outside what a pass freezes? CONCRETELY: may sit/dawnspur-line/index.html:419 and its
  byte-identical copy under public/ be changed in place from ", stake " + c.stake to ", " +
  (c.condition === null ? stakeText(c) : c.condition) — the parity form, which mirrors the
  board's own visible face at index.html:404; note this is NOT storm's bare stakeText(c),
  which would restore the stake and still speak the wrong thing on a sendable-but-unlit card
  — with sha256 b6f21db0… re-hashed in the same commit across 53 lines: the hub card
  (public/index.html:87), sit+public dawnspur-line/MANIFEST.txt:102, 16 full-sha pin lines
  and 6 short-sha list entries in 8 descendant test files, 18 lineage lines in 9 other
  boards' sit+public MANIFESTs, and 10 lines in 9 signed beat documents? AND IF YES: is the
  licence a standing rule (spoken text is outside the freeze, for every board, decided by
  whom per case) or a one-time exception for this line — and after the re-pin, is the board
  still PASSED, or does the corrected board have to be sat again at a new sibling path? WHY A
  YES REACHES FURTHER THAN ONE LINE: all eight descendant tests assert the lineage lock by
  name, seven of them for boards that have PASSED, and those boards' own signed beats forbid
  the overwrite in their own words — "Overwrite any live board." (cfd-205-beat.md:246,
  cfd-205-halt-beat.md:269), "Overwrite any live pin." (cfd-206:213, cfd-207:241,
  cfd-208:278), "is a PASSED board and does not get overwritten" (cfd-208:321, cfd-209:22).
  The sentence "Any existing board's bytes are touched" belongs to this board's own beat and
  its parent's (cfd-203-beat.md:1020, cfd-201-beat.md:1002), not to the descendants. A yes
  amends what all of those kill lines mean.
- *Action:* [record] Write the ruling into entry 42 IN PLACE — the existing entry in the
  decisions ledger, never a new document — as "RULED BY COUNSEL ON DAVID'S INSTRUCTION,
  2026-09-09: (b), leave the bytes and record the defect", carrying (i) the exact parity
  patch (condition-or-stakeText, NOT storm's bare stakeText(c)), (ii) the CORRECTED pin
  census — 53 lines: hub card public/index.html:87; 16 full-sha lines and 6 short-sha entries
  across 8 descendant tests; sit+public dawnspur-line/MANIFEST.txt:102; 18 lineage lines
  across 9 other boards' MANIFESTs; 10 lines across 9 signed beats — with SEVEN of the eight

  the correction that the entry's option (a) as written is a partial fix, and (iv) the

## 46. The rendered name of the Halt — a destination on the desk, home in the city, both on the storm — re-voice three passed card faces, or carry it as a declared row once a labels instrument exists?

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a design call.)*
On the desk boards "DAWNSPUR HALT" is a destination card you send a train to; on the city
boards "the Halt" is home — seven boards' spoken labels name it so, and it is the place
every send leaves from; three boards' pages (heat, scale and halt) are even titled
"Dawnspur Halt"; and the storm board shows both senses in one line — do you re-voice the
three passed desk boards' card faces so the name stops meaning two things, or leave the
name and carry the split as a declared row once the test guard can grade rendered words?

- *Why:* Every word is correct on its own board, so the confusion only appears to someone
  who reads across the two lineages. The guard that catches name collisions reads only
  element ids, toggles and class names today; a reader-facing label like this cannot be
  graded until a labels-and-sentences instrument is built, which is why the finding sits as a
  paragraph in a record rather than as a test.
- *Options:*
  a. **Re-voice the desk card faces** — The three passed desk boards are edited so the
     destination card no longer reads as the same place the city calls home. *Costs:* A large
     edit — three passed boards changed — to remove a word that is correct on each of them on
     its own board.
  b. **Carry it as a declared row once a labels instrument exists** — The name stays; the
     split is recorded as a known two-meaning row, gradeable only after the guard learns to read
     rendered labels and sentences. *Costs:* Until that instrument lands, the finding is a
     record entry and nothing more, and no test goes red for it.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator would build the labels
  instrument first — test-only work that makes the split gradeable — and decide the voice
  afterwards, rather than editing three passed boards now. *Yours:* Whether a desk card face
  should ever say anything other than the place's real name is a design call about how the
  world speaks, and it is yours.
- *If undecided:* The one finding both reviewers rated most serious stays a paragraph in a
  record, and the next board that names the Halt in a label picks whichever sense its author
  happens to hold.

*(Added 2026-09-08 from the DOM namespace sweep, call 5 — `docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:2005`;
finding 1, the sweep's only agreed HIGH, at `:1277`. Placed in Tier 2: it publishes one name
with two meanings to every reader of the pages.)*

**Question.** `<title>Dawnspur Halt</title>` heads heat, scale and halt; `DAWNSPUR HALT` is a
destination card face on dispatch, line and storm; *the Halt* is HOME in seven city
`aria-label`s (still-standing included); the storm renders both senses in one line. This is
the audit's #4 (`halt` as a route on the desk and home in the city) one layer out, in the
words a player reads. Is the desk's card face to be re-voiced — a board edit on three passed
boards — or is the split carried as a declared row once a `labels` / `sentences` deriver
exists in the guard?

**What the document says.** Finding 1 at `:1277`; the instrument-extensions bullet under
*Dispositions* ranks a `labels` deriver sixth and says that until it lands "finding 1 is a
record entry and nothing more." `ROWS.halt` (HIGH) grades ids only.

**What source says.** The `<title>` elements, the desk boards' card-face text, and the city
boards' `aria-label` attributes named above, all at `0ffd1aa`.

**Blocked.** A guard row for the sweep's one HIGH: the test loop's set list is hard-coded to
ids / toggles / classNames, so a `labels` row cannot go red today and is not built.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Build the instrument before deciding the voice.* A re-voicing of
three passed desk boards to remove a word that is correct on each of them, on its own board,
is a large edit to settle a cross-lineage confusion; a `labels` deriver plus the hard-coded
set list widened is test-only work and makes the split gradeable, after which the row carries
the two meanings the way `home` does. Whether the desk's card face should ever say something
other than the place's name is a design call, and yours.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** The one HIGH the sweep found stays a paragraph in a record, and
the next board that names the Halt in a label does so in whichever sense its author happens to
hold.

**RULED IN PART — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09; the rest RETURNED TO
DAVID.** RULED BY COUNSEL ON DAVID'S INSTRUCTION, 2026-09-09. Option (b): the rendered name
stays, no board byte moves, and the split is carried as a declared row once a
labels/sentences deriver exists in the guard. Option (a) is REFUSED AS AN ACT and PUT TO
DAVID AS A QUESTION - those are not the same thing, and the superseded revision withheld
the question, which the standing rules do not permit. AMENDMENTS, three, replacing the
three in the superseded revision. (1) THE ROW RECORDS ONE NAME AND TWO BOARD-MODELS, AND
DOES NOT SETTLE THE WORLD. The superseded revision ordered the row to record 'one place
seen from two vantages'. That publishes as settled what David's own signed and passed beat
calls two meanings: docs/cfd-210-beat.md:382-384 - 'Two boards, one word, two meanings:
home in one model, a place you send to in the other' - and :377-378 refuses the fork
because 'Send her to the Halt' means 'sending her where she already is' (SIGNED at :159,
PASSED at :6). The World Bible settles what the PLACE is - 'Dawnspur Halt | Verdant edge |
Core (becoming) | Home base | The player's restored station-town'
(C:/dev/skyrail/docs/lore/weekend-worldbuilding-2026-06-29/source/Skyrail-Reclamation-World-Bible-v0.1.md:353;
and Skyrail-Reclamation-Master-Design-Bible.md:92, 'Dawnspur Halt is home base.') - it does
not settle whether the desk model's send-to route is that same place, which is exactly what

docs/cfd-201-beat.md:1052 and :1442, which call the destination 'the home halt', as the

(docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:238) - traced at :262-268 to

0.08, description 'The home halt, newly awake, with warm lamps, market gardens, and the

PASSED) - Two boards, one word, two meanings: home in one model, a place you send to in the
other - and the world files the place itself as home base (World-Bible-v0.1.md:353;
Master-Design-Bible.md:92). The pack's own gloss The home halt
(C:/dev/skyrail/src/content.js:381) is the recorded bridge, not this row's wording. This
field is ungraded prose (test/lexicon.test.js:38-41).' (2) FINDING 1 STAYS HIGH AND
UNGRADED until instrument extension 6 lands - docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:1723-1725: 'Until
(6) lands, finding 1 is a record entry and nothing more, and the record says so rather than
proposing a decorative row.' The labels row must not be declared before the deriver:
declared today it lands RED, refused by name at test/lexicon.test.js:606-620. The council
does not order the deriver - it is already owed, ranked sixth in the sweep's own
Dispositions, and the orchestrator may schedule that test-only work on its own authority.
(3) FOUR MEASURED CORRECTIONS RIDE WITH THE RULING, each stated exactly as measured, per
the ambient contract's verify-before-reporting and supersede-in-place rules. (i) The
entry's 'three passed desk boards' (:7-8, :17, :19, :42, :57) is FALSE: dispatch passed its
sit 2026-08-26 and line passed its sit 2026-08-28 (public/index.html:91, :85); storm is
'live - sat five times, stopped without a pass' (public/index.html:79; KILLS.md:68-88,
'recut 5 was never sat'). The lineage lock covers passed and killed alike, so this does not
make (a) available. (ii) The entry's 'the storm renders both senses in one line' is HALF
MEASURED AND HALF UNPROVEN, and the superseded revision was wrong to mark the whole premise
UNMEASURED. MEASURED BY THIS COUNCIL at e226a9f, read-only, from the opening the board
itself mints (marks 3, stores 0, sky clear; the roll is stubbed only for the run outcome
and the sky is turn-driven at SKY_PERIOD 9, sim.js:123-126, so no state is forced): the
gestures send halt / meet, three times, yield at the third send the single #say string 'The
train left for Dawnspur Halt with nothing staked. A stormbird is inland over the halt.
There is a storm coming in behind it.' runSentence is spoken(), which joins s.sentence and
s.skySentence with a space (sit/dawnspur-storm/sim.js:657-661, :701), and
index.html:581-582 writes it into #say as one text node. The co-occurrence in one rendered
line is reachable and now measured. UNPROVEN, and this is the half the ledger must mark:
that the second occurrence carries the HOME sense. On the same board sim.js:139 gives the
route's own agent line as 'A shear in the near line short of the halt.' - lowercase 'the
halt' as the DESTINATION - and sim.js:130 names the desk's own ground as 'The storm is over
Dawnspur. The sun is off the terrace and the basin road is dark.', never as the halt. ALSO
MEASURED, and new in this revision: the MEET that follows renders, in one line, 'A shear in
the near line short of the halt. The train turned for home with the haul unbanked. The free
hop staked nothing and nothing comes back; the route paid nothing; the train is home, and
the desk stands.' - lowercase 'the halt' as destination and 'the train is home' in the same
string, which is direct evidence against reading the weather's lowercase halt as the desk's
own ground. The refuter's 'the weather's halt is where the desk stands' (sweep :1136-1138)
is one reading, and the escalation from 'three surfaces, one word' to 'self-contradictory
on a single board' rests on it. (iii) No board carries both senses as page identity AND
destination. The three boards whose sim.js carries the CARD-FACE string 'DAWNSPUR HALT' are
dispatch, line and storm, titled Dawnspur Switchyard, Dawnspur Line and Dawnspur Storm
(index.html:6 on each); the three boards titled <title>Dawnspur Halt</title> - heat, scale
and halt - carry that CARD-FACE string zero times in sim.js or index.html, and carry no
ROUTES at all (grep -c ROUTES = 0 on each sim.js, measured at e226a9f). Say card-face
string: the superseded revision said those boards 'carry the string zero times', which is
falsifiable by its own reader, since 'Dawnspur Halt' IS their title (index.html:6) and sits
in a comment at sit/dawnspur-halt/sim.js:3. (iv) The sweep's 'an unknown set key is
silently skipped' is AMENDED, not superseded: the drift loop still iterates the literal ids
/ toggles / classNames and still skips an unknown key in silence
(test/lexicon.test.js:553); the separate can-go-red test (:606-620) now REFUSES the
declaration by name - 'set labels is not one the derivation produces (ids / toggles /
classNames) - the drift loop skips it in silence, so this half of the row grades nothing' -
so such a row lands RED rather than green. WHY (b) AND NOT (a): (a) edits the shipped,
hash-pinned bytes of /dawnspur-dispatch/ and /dawnspur-line/ (passed) and of
/dawnspur-storm/ (stopped without a pass), against the lineage lock at
docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:406-409 - 'Never overwrite a passed or killed board. Every
cumulative sitting ships at a new sibling path, and the boards it inherits from stay live
and untouched at their own paths' - and it re-pins the hub card and every descendant
(dispatch sim 576ce2b6 appears in 11 test files, 13 docs, the hub card graded by
test/boards-index.test.js:90, and 22 MANIFEST.txt files: 11 boards, each with its sit/
original and its served public/ twin - measured with grep -rl over --include=MANIFEST.txt
at e226a9f). David's 2026-09-01 disposition, under the heading 'Dispositions - RULED,
David, 2026-09-01' (docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:792), routes the analogous
source edits to passed boards 'to the next board's cut, where they are a design call for
its beat, not a cleanup' (:851-853) - corroborative; the lock alone is dispositive. (b)
costs zero shipped bytes, is fully reversible, and cannot be faked: the can-go-red test
refuses a decorative row. *(majority; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic —
one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in
this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named under Carried out
moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* THREE OF FOUR COUNSELLORS CHOSE (b) - reader, canon and cost/reversibility - and
  the fourth, the skeptic, chose return-to-David with an interim of 'no change to any board
  or ledger byte'. On the operative half - do not re-voice, move no shipped bytes - the
  council is unanimous, and all four independently drafted the exact question option (a)
  would require David to answer. THIS IS A REVISION. An adversarial check refuted the first
  consensus; every one of its nine defects reproduces from the record. The moderator
  re-opened each source and settled it by quoting, and re-ran the one disputed measurement
  rather than reasoning about it. WHAT CHANGED AND WHY. (A) THE FIRST REVISION'S AMENDMENT
  (1) IS WITHDRAWN. It ordered the declared row to record 'ONE place seen from two vantages,
  not two places', citing the World Bible and cfd-201's 'your own halt'. It cited neither of
  the two sources that govern: David's signed and passed cfd-210:382-384 says 'Two boards,
  one word, two meanings: home in one model, a place you send to in the other', and :377-378
  refuses 'Send her to the Halt' as 'sending her where she already is'. A row worded 'one
  place' publishes as settled what a signed beat calls two meanings - and it would do so in
  ROWS.halt's meaning field, which test/lexicon.test.js:38-41 records as ungraded prose where
  'four meaning sentences were false under a green guard until the twin drives landed'. A
  fifth false sentence there, written by a council ruling, is the exact failure this project
  keeps paying for. (B) THE FIRST REVISION'S PRESCRIBED DESK WORDING WOULD HAVE WRITTEN THE
  BRIDGE INTO THE GUARD. It directed ROWS.halt's desk side to read 'the free hop whose
  destination is the home halt'. That phrase is what the record files as the DEFECT:
  docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:238 - 'docs/cfd-201-beat.md:1052 calls the
  destination the home halt - the design record itself hands the next author the bridge' -
  traced at :262-268 to C:/dev/skyrail/src/content.js:369-382 and classed at :1045 as 'PWA
  pack -> DESK - not built'. The existing row already reads better than the proposed
  replacement: 'a destination the train is sent TO' (test/lexicon-ledger.js:697). The
  amendment now adds a cited note and leaves the operative sentence alone. (C) THE
  MEASUREMENT. The first revision marked the entry's 'both senses in one line' premise
  UNMEASURED. It is not. Run at e226a9f from the minted opening, three send-halt/meet pairs
  produce the single string 'The train left for Dawnspur Halt with nothing staked. A
  stormbird is inland over the halt. There is a storm coming in behind it.' Publishing a
  correction the board falsifies in one node run would have broken the first rule of the
  ambient contract inside a ruling that invokes it. What survives, and is now stated as the
  unproven half only, is the sense the second occurrence carries - and the same run yields
  new evidence for that half: the MEET line renders 'A shear in the near line short of the
  halt ... the train is home, and the desk stands' in ONE string, so on this board lowercase
  'the halt' is the destination while 'home' is where the desk stands. (D) THE OPTION-(a)
  QUESTION IS PUT, NOT WITHHELD. The first revision wrote 'Separately, and NOT asked as a
  request ... the council refuses it rather than putting it to you.' The standing rules say
  an option that edits a passed board's pinned bytes is reported as REQUIRES DAVID with the
  exact question he must answer, with the best in-rules option as the interim. Refusing to
  PERFORM (a) is right and is unchanged; deciding that David is not asked is not the
  council's call, and all four counsellors had drafted the question. (E) TWO PUBLISHED
  FIGURES CORRECTED, ONE WORDING MADE FALSIFIABLE-PROOF. '11 MANIFESTs' is 22 files at
  e226a9f - 11 boards, sit/ original plus served public/ twin (the cost lens wrote '(sit/ and
  served twins)'; the consensus dropped the parenthetical). 'Master Design Bible :92' now
  carries its full path. And 'the three boards titled Dawnspur Halt carry the string zero
  times' is corrected to the CARD-FACE string 'DAWNSPUR HALT', because those boards' titles
  are literally 'Dawnspur Halt' (index.html:6 on heat, scale and halt) and
  sit/dawnspur-halt/sim.js:3 carries it in a comment. (F) THE SUPERSESSION IS AMENDMENT, NOT
  SUPERSESSION. The drift loop at test/lexicon.test.js:553 still skips unknown keys silently;
  what changed is that a second test refuses the declaration. Saying 'superseded' would trade
  one imprecision for another. WHAT DID NOT CHANGE, AND WHY IT SURVIVED THE CHECK: (b) over
  (a) on the lineage lock at docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:406-409, corroborated by David's
  own ruled disposition at name-collisions-audit:792 and :851-853; the finding staying HIGH
  and ungraded until extension 6; the deriver being owed work the orchestrator may schedule
  rather than a fourth mechanism the council invents; zero bytes under sit/ or public/; and
  the observation that no boards-side option in entry 46 reaches the upstream origin at
  src/content.js:381, so re-voicing three card faces would remove the symptom on three boards
  and leave the source every future desk author imports from.
- *Dissent:* THE SKEPTIC'S RETURN-TO-DAVID is the strongest dissent, and in this revision
  it is stronger than the first consensus allowed. THE FIRST CONSENSUS BURIED ITS BEST FORM.
  It said the skeptic 'lost on its own first horn' because 'the record answers that without
  him', and then never put the two sources that make the horn strong against that answer:
  David's signed and passed cfd-210:382-384 ('Two boards, one word, two meanings'), and the
  audit's filing of cfd-201's 'the home halt' as the bridge rather than as evidence of intent
  (name-collisions-audit:238, :262-268, :1045). With both on the table the horn does not
  lose: the world question is genuinely open, and this revision puts it to David rather than
  answering it for him. What the skeptic still does not win is the disposition. Its own
  interim was 'no change to any board or ledger byte - option (b)'s substance without its
  promise', and that is narrower than the record supports: the entry contains a false figure
  ('three passed desk boards'), a half-unmeasured premise, and a superseded description of
  the guard, and the ambient contract's supersede-in-place rule makes correcting those an
  obligation, not an option. This ruling therefore GOES PAST the dissenter's interim and
  moves ledger bytes - docs/decisions-open-2026-09-02.md, docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md, and prose
  in test/lexicon-ledger.js - while moving no byte under sit/ or public/. That is recorded
  here rather than glossed, because the first consensus did not record it. The skeptic also
  won the boundary it named: the council may not order the instrument as a fourth mechanism;
  the ruling records it as owed test-only work already ranked sixth in the sweep's
  Dispositions. The canon lens's amendment - that both senses name one place - is NOT adopted
  as the row's wording, for the reason in the rationale, but its underlying evidence (World
  Bible :353, Master Design Bible :92) is carried into the row as a cited note and into
  question (1) for David. The reader lens's case - that the reader who inherits the error is
  the next beat author, not the player - is the reason the row is worth declaring at all, and
  is unchanged.
- *Requires David:* FOUR QUESTIONS, none blocking this ruling, all for the NEXT desk cut.
  (1) THE WORLD, put and NOT answered for him: 'Your signed and passed cfd-210 says: Two
  boards, one word, two meanings - home in one model, a place you send to in the other
  (docs/cfd-210-beat.md:382-384), and it refuses Send her to the Halt as sending her where
  she already is (:377-378). Your World Bible files Dawnspur Halt as Home base, the player's
  restored station-town (World-Bible-v0.1.md:353), and the Master Design Bible says Dawnspur
  Halt is home base (Master-Design-Bible.md:92). Meanwhile the three desk boards send a train
  to a route card reading DAWNSPUR HALT while their own pages are titled Dawnspur Switchyard,
  Dawnspur Line and Dawnspur Storm. Is the desk's route the same place as the city's home,
  seen from one hop out - or a distinct stop that happens to share the name? The declared row
  will record whichever you say; it will not decide it, and until you do it records one name
  and two board-models.' (2) THE VOICE: 'If it is the same place, should a desk destination
  card ever render the home's own name - and if it is to be re-voiced, to what? A re-voicing
  ships only in the next desk cut, at a new sibling path, under a signed beat and your sit -
  never on these three.' (3) OPTION (a) ITSELF, put as the standing rules require rather than
  withheld: 'Option (a) as written cannot be done inside the rules. It would need you to
  reverse the lineage lock (docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:406-409, Never overwrite a passed
  or killed board) and your own 2026-09-01 disposition
  (docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:792, :851-853) for /dawnspur-dispatch/ (passed
  2026-08-26), /dawnspur-line/ (passed 2026-08-28) and /dawnspur-storm/ (stopped without a
  pass). The council refuses to do it, and recommends you do not authorise it: the record
  shows nothing that needs it, it re-pins the hub card and 22 MANIFEST.txt files, and it
  would leave the origin standing at src/content.js:381. Do you want it anyway?' (4) THE
  UPSTREAM CAUSE, which no option in entry 46 reaches: 'src/content.js:369-382 files the home
  under ROUTES as id dawnspur-halt, baseRisk 0.08, description The home halt, newly awake,
  with warm lamps, market gardens, and the first working platform. Any future desk author who
  imports route ids re-derives the split, and your cfd-201 beat quotes that gloss twice
  (:1052, :1442), which the audit files as the bridge itself. Re-voice or re-file it in the
  PWA repo, or does the pack keep home as a route?'

# Tier 3 — Convention and register; measurement does not decide these (18; ruled by counsel 2026-09-09: 13 outright, 5 in part and returned to David)

## 20. The Canon check section — retroactive on the seven beats that lack one?

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a yes/no.)*
Do the seven beats that have no Canon check section get one added after the fact, or does
the rule that every beat cites the rulebook bind only beats written from now on?

- *Why:* The rulebook says every beat must carry a Canon check section citing which of its
  ten rules, or which source line, each mechanism rests on, and a beat with an uncited
  mechanism is refused at review before you see it. Seven beats have no such section (the
  heat board's beat, the halt board's Come-home beat, the Mosswake, Herbs-in-the-larder and
  They-remember beats, the dice beat and the Two-ways-from-here beat); six do, so today the
  corpus obeys the rule about half the time.
- *Options:*
  a. **Retroactive** — Someone writes a Canon check section into each of the seven beats,
     citing rules for mechanisms that were designed and played without those citations. *Costs:*
     Seven files of invented provenance on boards that already passed, and one trap: adding a
     heat citation to the heat board's beat silently picks one side of a rulebook contradiction
     about how that board grades WARM (open decision 12), which you have not ruled on yet.
  b. **Not retroactive** — The seven beats stay as they are; the citation rule binds only
     beats written from here forward. *Costs:* A canon-check discipline that only half the
     corpus obeys, so a reader cannot rely on finding one.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator reads it as not retroactive:
  back-filling citations nobody used at the time is archaeology, and the value is the
  convention going forward. *Yours:* Whether the rulebook rule is a going-forward convention
  or a corpus-wide requirement is yours; the heat-board sweep lists it as an action for you.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked. The seven beats simply stay uncited, and if anyone
  adds a citation to the heat board's beat before you rule on decision 12, they pick a side
  of that contradiction for you.
- *What the entry does not say:* The entry frames these as beats written before the
  citation rule, but by first-commit date only the heat board's beat is as old as the rule
  (both 2026-08-25); the other six were first committed 2026-08-30 to 2026-09-01, after it,
  and the entry does not say why they were written without the section. The repo today also
  holds a newer beat (Still standing, cfd-212) with none, which the entry predates.

**Question.** Yes or no: do beats written before the convention get a Canon check section
added retroactively?

**What the audit says.** `docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:1274-1275`: *"no Canon
check section on 205-halt, 206, 207, 208 (retroactive or not)"*. The CFD-176 sweep raised
the same thing independently as row `M6`, UNCITED, action DAVID — *"the whole beat: no
Canon check section, zero R-rule citations"*.

**What source says.** Measured at HEAD, `grep -c 'Canon check' docs/cfd-*.md`: **seven**
beats lack one, not four — `cfd-176`, `cfd-205-halt`, `cfd-206`, `cfd-207`, `cfd-208`,
`cfd-209`, `cfd-210`. Six have one (`183`, `196`, `200` ×2, `201`, `203`, `205`). **The
audit's list of four is short by three.** Corrected here.

**Blocked.** Nothing. But note the coupling: if you add a citation to `cfd-176`, entry #12
must be ruled first — the sweep warns that citing *"Heat vs Air — R9"* silently picks canon
`:118` over `:105`.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Not retroactive.* Seven files of back-fill on boards that already
passed, adding citations nobody used at the time, is archaeology; the convention going
forward is the value.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** Either seven beats of invented provenance, or a canon-check
discipline that only half the corpus obeys.

**RULED IN PART — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09; the rest RETURNED TO
DAVID.** No back-fill — as an INTERIM, recorded as 'no back-fill' and explicitly NOT as a
narrowing of §6.1, with the reserved question carried to David rather than dissolved. The
adversarial check refuted this council's central finding and the refutation holds on
re-measurement, so the finding is restated honestly: the heading-grep is still the wrong
instrument, but the section it was looking for is genuinely absent from more beats than the
first consensus admitted. Measured at e226a9f: eight beats carry no 'Canon check' heading —
cfd-176, cfd-205-halt, cfd-206, cfd-207, cfd-208, cfd-209, cfd-210, cfd-212-still-standing.
Four of those eight carry a real section under a second name: 'Canon this sitting' as a
heading at cfd-208:408, cfd-209:196, cfd-210:499, cfd-212:821. Three carry the same words
as an unheaded preamble inside the signed beat — cfd-205-halt:75, cfd-206:59, cfd-207:72 —
and cfd-176 carries neither section nor preamble nor any R-cite. But §6.1's standard is
per-MECHANISM, not per-beat, and the first consensus answered a different question: canon
:250-251, 'Each mechanism the beat turns ON or REFUSES cites the rule above (R1-R10) or the
source line it rests on', re-bound by §7's 'What does NOT change' at :411-413, 'The canon
check. §6.1 binds every beat, cumulative or not: every mechanism turned ON or REFUSED cites
its rule or source line. An inherited system is cited to the beat that passed it.' Measured
against that: cfd-209 (9 R-cites, and a From column naming §7.1, §7, 'CFD-205 halt,
PASSED', 'CFD-196 / CFD-203', 'CFD-201 ruling 5 / CFD-203, via §7.2' — the exact form
:412-413 asks for), cfd-210 (4) and cfd-212 (8) meet it. cfd-206, cfd-207 and cfd-208 carry
ZERO R-cites and their 'On this sitting' From cells give bare 'inherited', bare 'signed'
and 'pins' — cfd-206:174 '| **Works — Foundry is work one** | **ON as look** — already live
| inherited |', cfd-206:176 '| **Held island is not a fuel bill** | **ON** | inherited |',
cfd-206:181-186 six consecutive REFUSED rows citing 'signed', cfd-207:198-200 and :204-213
the same, cfd-208:227-229 and :234-244 the same. A bare 'signed' points at the beat's own
signed brief and a bare 'inherited' points at nothing; neither is a rule or a source line.
The audit itself read it this way at docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:1211-1214:
'cfd-206 carries no Canon check section and zero R-rule citations on a beat signed five
days after §6.1 landed; none of cfd-205-halt, 206, 207, 208 has one (209 and 210 carry
"Canon this sitting").' So the honest finding is: the discipline is met on cfd-209, cfd-210
and cfd-212 under a second heading name; it is NOT met per-mechanism on cfd-176,
cfd-205-halt, cfd-206, cfd-207 and cfd-208. No back-fill is ordered anyway, for two reasons
the council can stand behind rather than the one it gave: cfd-176 is barred until entry 12
is ruled — that entry's own Blocked line warns that citing 'Heat vs Air — R9' silently
picks canon :118 over :105 — and back-filling citations onto four further beats of boards
that already passed is the archaeology option (a) itself prices as 'invented provenance'.
Going forward, new beats use canon's own heading, 'Canon check', with a per-mechanism
'Rests on' column in the shape of cfd-205:270-285. And because §6.1 is ruled text the
council may not narrow, whether David's signatures over those five beats stand as his
waiver is his call, not this council's: it is asked, not dismissed. *(majority; four lenses
— canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one
revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the
council read; the landing named under Carried out moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* REVISED after an adversarial check. The check's first two findings are MATERIAL
  and are adopted; two of its smaller findings are refuted from the record and the original
  stands there. Everything below was re-opened and re-quoted at e226a9f. 1) WHAT THE CHECK
  GOT RIGHT, AND IT IS THE HEART OF THE RULING. The first consensus said the seven
  heading-less beats 'carry the same section under a second name ... each citing a rule or a
  source line, which is §6.1's own standard'. That conflates a beat-level sources PREAMBLE
  with §6.1's per-mechanism requirement, and it is refuted by the beats' own tables. Opened
  and quoted: cfd-206:59-63 is a paragraph inside '## The signed beat' reading 'Canon this
  sitting (cite, do not recall as a second bible): Tutorial Beat 5-7. World Bible §12
  Mosswake Loop. Core Loop: nodes and lines...' — a list of what the beat read, not a
  mapping. The mapping table is elsewhere, at cfd-206:170-194, and its From column reads
  'inherited', 'signed', 'pins', 'site/halt fails'. cfd-207:192-215 and cfd-208:220-245 are
  the same shape. Compare cfd-205:272-285, a real Canon check table with Row / State / 'Rests
  on' and R1, R9, R10, §7 in the third column. The gate did not fire in substance on cfd-206,
  cfd-207 or cfd-208. The canon lens's dissent was answered with a measurement of a different
  question — the exact instrument error the ruling itself diagnosed two paragraphs earlier —
  and it is corrected here rather than defended. 2) THE SCOPE IS SMALLER THAN THE FIRST
  CONSENSUS CLAIMED AND LARGER THAN 'ONE BEAT'. Re-measured heading counts at e226a9f, per
  beat: cfd-176 0; cfd-183 1; cfd-196 3; cfd-200 2; cfd-201 1; cfd-203 1; cfd-205 1;
  cfd-205-halt 0; cfd-206 0; cfd-207 0; cfd-208 0; cfd-209 0; cfd-210 0; cfd-212 0. 'Canon
  this sitting' as a HEADING exists at cfd-208:408 ('### Canon this sitting'), cfd-209:196,
  cfd-210:499, cfd-212:821 — four beats. As an unheaded preamble sentence at cfd-205-halt:75,
  cfd-206:59, cfd-207:72, cfd-208:81. R-cite counts: cfd-209 9, cfd-212 8, cfd-210 4,
  cfd-205-halt 2, cfd-176 / 206 / 207 / 208 zero. So five beats fail §6.1 per-mechanism, not
  one. 3) THE CITE THE CHECK SAYS IS OWED, PAID. §6's own heading at canon :244 carries no
  RULED marker — the cost lens flagged this independently and entry 6 records §§1-4 unsigned,
  canon :12 reading 'David signs this doc before any recut implements it'. What makes §6.1
  binding is §7, whose heading is at :263 and whose RULED marker is at :265 ('**RULED —
  David, 2026-08-26.**'), carrying §6.1 forward at :411-413. A reader checking §6 alone finds
  no ruling; the ruling rests on :411-413 and says so. 4) TWO CITES CORRECTED. The
  LEGACY-bible instruction is at cfd-207:86-87 ('World Bible v0.1 is LEGACY except / §8
  (sections 2, 4, 6). Do not cite World Bible §12 as live canon.'), not :83-85;
  cfd-208:105-107 was right. And cfd-206's World Bible §12 citation is at :60, not :59. 5)
  TWO ACTIONS WITHDRAWN. The first consensus instructed the heading fix 'in the beat template
  and the worker brief'. No such artefact exists in this repository — docs/ holds fourteen
  beats, the ledger, canon, the audit and the sweep, and no template — so that instruction is
  not carryable and is dropped; the convention is recorded in the ledger instead. And the
  instruction to record that 'Canon this sitting' names the same section is dropped as false
  per (1): recording it would install the mis-measurement rather than correct it. 6) WHY THE
  CHOICE SURVIVES THE CORRECTION. The corrected finding strengthens the canon lens's dissent
  on the facts and still does not carry it to option (a). Option (a) on cfd-176 decides entry
  12 sideways, which every lens agreed is out of the council's authority. Option (a) on
  cfd-205-halt, 206, 207 and 208 writes rule citations, in 2026-09, for mechanisms designed
  and played in 2026-08 by authors who did not use them — and §6's own stated value is the
  timing, not the words: the sanction is 'refused at PM review — before David ever sees it',
  a gate at the moment of review that cannot now be applied to a beat David has already
  signed and a board he has already passed. What is left, and it is the honest residual, is
  that four passed beats stand outside a ruled requirement. That is not the council's to
  waive, so it is asked.
- *Dissent:* The canon lens, whose position is materially strengthened by this revision. It
  chose (a), scoped: 'every beat' is ruled text and reading it as forward-only adds a clause
  David did not sign; six of the eight were first committed after §6.1 landed 2026-08-25, so
  this is a review gate that was not run, not a rule reaching backward. In the first
  consensus it lost on a measurement that has now been refuted — four of its five targets
  were said to already carry the section, and cfd-206, cfd-207 and cfd-208 do not carry it in
  §6.1's sense at all. It survives on the facts and still loses on the remedy, for reasons it

  itself unsigned per entry 6, so the ruling rests on §7:411-413 and not on §6's own

- *Requires David:* Two questions, the first restored from the skeptic lens after the

- *Action:* [record] Correct entry 20's 'What source says' in the ledger, on both counts.

## 21. The `rim` alias note — 206 alone, or all three?

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: pick one of two or three.)*
Does the note explaining that the rim is the halt board's own constant and is not read on
the three sibling boards go on the Herbs-in-the-larder (Put them up) and They-remember
(Collect) beats too, or stay only on the Mosswake (Send) beat?

- *Why:* The word rim means two things across the seam between the halt board and its three
  sibling boards. The Mosswake beat already carries a note disambiguating it, while the other
  two beats still say only that the rim/gap is a constant the action does not write, with no
  note.
- *Options:*
  a. **All three** — Carry the same disambiguation note on the Herbs-in-the-larder and
     They-remember beats as well; those two edits are already written and waiting. *Costs:*
     Landing the two edits that are already built; the entry names nothing beyond that.
  b. **Mosswake beat only** — Leave the note where it is; the other two beats keep their
     one-line constant statement. *Costs:* Two beats keep an ambiguous constant that the third
     beat explicitly disambiguates, and a reader of either is left wrong.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator reads it as all three: the note
  exists because the word is ambiguous across the seam, and fixing one of three sites is the
  half-fix that leaves two readers wrong. *Yours:* Whether the two sibling beats need the
  note at all, or whether the Mosswake beat's is enough, is the register call the entry
  leaves to you.
- *If undecided:* Two built edits stay on hold, as they have since the start of September,
  and the two beats stay ambiguous.

**Question.** Pick one: does the `rim` disambiguation note go on `cfd-207` and `cfd-208`
too, or stay on `cfd-206` only?

**What the document says.** `docs/cfd-206-beat.md:223` carries it: *"rim — `rim` is
`/dawnspur-halt/`'s own constant and is not read here. SEND does … constant"; the rim is
halt's, and this board carries only the gap.)*"

**What source says.** Measured at HEAD *(e226a9f; both bullets were replaced on 2026-09-09 under counsel's ruling on this entry — see the ruling below)*: `cfd-207:251` reads *"**Rim / gap is a constant.**
Put them up does not write it."* and `cfd-208:288` reads *"**Rim / gap is a constant.**
Collect does not write it."* — **neither carries the note.** The audit's cite
`cfd-208:278` has drifted to `:288`; corrected here. Both edits are built and held:
`docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:1248-1250`, *"the two conditional `rim` edits"*.

**Blocked.** Two built edits, held since 2026-09-01.

**Orchestrator's read.** *All three.* The note exists because the word means two things
across the seam; carrying it on one of three sites is the half-fix that leaves the other
two readers wrong.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** Two beats keep an ambiguous constant that the third
explicitly disambiguates.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** All three — the disambiguation
note goes onto cfd-207:251 and cfd-208:288 in cfd-206:223-226's form, with each board's own
verb. The choice is unchanged; two things in the first consensus's reasoning were refuted
at adversarial review and are corrected, and one of them changes the words that get
written. Re-measured at e226a9f, `grep -rn 'const RIM' sit/` returns TWO declarations, not
one: sit/dawnspur-halt/sim.js:39 and sit/dawnspur-site/sim.js:27, both `const RIM =
Object.freeze({ left: 78, width: 18 })`. So the landed cfd-206 note's clause 'rim is
`/dawnspur-halt/`'s own constant' names one owner where the corpus has two, and copying it
verbatim into two more beats would knowingly publish an unmeasured provenance claim — the
thing the ambient contract's first rule refuses. The load-bearing half is measured and
true: none of sit/mosswake-loop/sim.js, sit/herbs-larder/sim.js or sit/they-remember/sim.js
declares a rim; each declares only `const GAP = Object.freeze({ left: 42, width: 16 })`, at
:33, :36 and :57 respectively, and all three suites assert `postNotice('rim') === false` —
test/mosswake-loop.test.js:360, test/herbs-larder.test.js:389,
test/they-remember.test.js:437. So the note lands on all three with the provenance clause
made accurate, and the same one-clause correction is superseded in place on cfd-206:224 so
all three read alike — the reader lens's constraint (same words in all three, or a third
phrasing creates a fourth reading of the word) is satisfied by fixing three, not by copying
an inexact one twice. *(unanimous; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one
moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in
this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named under Carried out
moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* REVISED after an adversarial check. The choice was unanimous across all four
  lenses and is untouched; two of the check's three findings are adopted and one of them
  changes the prescribed wording. 1) THE MEASUREMENT THE PROPAGATED WORDING RESTS ON WAS
  WRONG. The first consensus said RIM 'exists only at sit/dawnspur-halt/sim.js:39'.
  Re-measured: `grep -rn 'const RIM' sit/` returns sit/dawnspur-halt/sim.js:39 AND
  sit/dawnspur-site/sim.js:27, byte-identical. The check is right, and it matters because the
  sentence being carried into two more files asserts exactly that uniqueness. The remedy is
  not to carry it verbatim and footnote the error in a ledger the beat's reader will never
  open; it is to write the measured clause into all three beats. That is one extra edit, in
  the established supersession form, on a note the orchestrator itself appended at 5ad4c6f —
  not a signed line, not a board byte. 2) POINT (5) OF THE FIRST CONSENSUS IS WITHDRAWN, AND
  IT WAS THE SAME INSTRUMENT ERROR THIS DOCKET KEEPS FINDING. It 'corrected' the audit's 'one
  template line in three beats and three test titles' by reporting that no test title reads
  'Rim / gap'. That grep was for the literal string 'Rim / gap' — capital R, spaced slash.
  The alias is in three test titles in the corpus's own casing:
  test/mosswake-loop.test.js:539, test/herbs-larder.test.js:563 and
  test/they-remember.test.js:640, each reading 'kill: Foundry does Heat look, not Air — Halt
  ground already stepped, rim/gap unmoved'. The audit at
  docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:1246 was right and the correction is withdrawn.
  What remains is a RESIDUAL, recorded rather than fixed: after the three beat edits the
  alias survives in three test titles on passed boards, and moving it there is a test edit
  this entry did not put on the table. 3) THE VERIFICATION IS RE-SCOPED TO THE GUARD THAT
  ACTUALLY GRADES BEAT PROSE. `grep -rln 'Rim / gap' test/` was the wrong instrument again.
  test/beats-lexicon.test.js is the docs half of the name-collision guard — its own header
  states it is 'the DOCS half ... test/lexicon.test.js guards the boards' name surface and
  says, in its own header, what it provably cannot catch: anything in docs/'. That is the
  suite the landing must be measured against, together with the three sibling board suites.
  The adversarial check reports running it at e226a9f — 11/11 at baseline and 11/11 with the
  two prescribed notes applied to a scratch corpus via SKYRAIL_BEATS_DIR. That is the check's
  measurement, attributed to it, not re-run here (this council is read-only); the landing
  worker re-runs it rather than trusting either report. 4) WHAT STANDS UNCHANGED. The
  template exists verbatim at cfd-206:223-226. The hold was not a ruling — the audit's

## 22. `cfd-205`'s INHERITED-as-scenery rows — hearth and greenhouse

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: pick one of two or three.)*
On the stopped site beat, is the keel-fire hearth inherited as scenery or lit together with
the Foundry, and is the greenhouse inherited as scenery or refused outright?

- *Why:* The site beat's inheritance table lists the hearth as inherited scenery from the
  heat board and the greenhouse as inherited scenery from the scale board, explicitly not a
  second greenhouse and not a build-up. The site board was sat and stopped rather than recut,
  so nothing is waiting on these rows today.
- *Options:*
  a. **Hearth as scenery** — The hearth stays an inert inherited fixture, as the row reads
     now. *Costs:* The entry names one cost for the whole pair of picks, not one per side: a
     later board inherits a row it should have refused, or refuses one it could have kept.
  b. **Hearth lit with the Foundry** — The hearth becomes part of the live work when the
     Foundry starts, not decoration. *Costs:* The entry names one cost for the whole pair of
     picks, not one per side: a later board inherits a row it should have refused, or refuses
     one it could have kept.
  c. **Greenhouse inherited as scenery** — The greenhouse stays a standing terrace job that
     is present but not played and not built up, as the row reads now. *Costs:* If this is the
     wrong call, a later board inherits a row it should have refused.
  d. **Greenhouse refused** — The row is struck as refused, so no later board can carry it
     from here. *Costs:* If this is the wrong call, a later board refuses a row it could have
     kept.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator reads it as leave both as
  inherited scenery: inert-versus-refused is a register call on a stopped board, and the
  present wording is at least consistent with what the heat and scale beats say. *Yours:* The
  entry says the distinction is a register call on a stopped board, and what a later board
  should be allowed to inherit is yours.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked; the site board was stopped, not recut. The risk is a
  later board inheriting a row it should have refused, or refusing one it could have kept.

**Question.** Two picks: is the hearth **"as scenery"** or **lit-with-the-Foundry**, and is
the greenhouse **INHERITED as scenery** or **REFUSED**?

**What the document says.** `docs/cfd-205-beat.md:214`: *"| Keel-fire / hearth as scenery |
INHERITED as scenery | CFD-176 |"*. `:215`: *"| Greenhouse as a standing terrace job |
INHERITED as scenery; not a second greenhouse and not UP | CFD-183 |"*. **The audit cited
these as `:207` and `:208`; both have drifted by seven lines.** Corrected here.

**Blocked.** Nothing; `/dawnspur-site/` was stopped, not recut.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Leave both as INHERITED-as-scenery.* The distinction between
"inherited but inert" and "refused" is a register call on a stopped board, and the current
wording is at least internally consistent with `cfd-176`'s and `cfd-183`'s.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** A later board inherits a row it should have refused, or
refuses one it could have kept.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** No change to either row, plus
one dated gloss. The hearth row stays 'INHERITED as scenery — CFD-176' at cfd-205:214 and
the greenhouse row stays 'INHERITED as scenery; not a second greenhouse and not UP —
CFD-183' at :215. The gloss records the byte fact that makes option (b) look tempting, so
the next reader does not re-find it and mistake it for a mechanism. The choice is
unchanged; the adversarial check found the ruling's first ground mis-cited and its action
unplaceable, and both are fixed. R5 is at docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:46-51, not :63-66
— :63-68 is R7, 'Fuel is inflection, not a standing bill' — and the sentence the ruling
rests on is at :50-51: 'The hearth's mercy is physics: "it is the first and smallest thing
to cross its own self-sustaining threshold."' R4 is at :36-44, with 'never touching the
hearth' at :40. And the corpus already files this exact row, so the ruling adopts that
citation rather than inventing one: cfd-183:243 reads '| Bank and hearth as standing
scenery | ON | R8 — the bank-in-the-stone stands as played (§3); R4 — the hearth is spared,
"never touching the hearth" |'. The gloss cannot go 'beside' a table row without breaking
the table, so it goes as a standalone paragraph after the table's last row at cfd-205:224,
naming the row it glosses. *(unanimous; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic
— one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites
in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named under Carried
out moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* REVISED after an adversarial check. Unanimous across all four lenses on the
  outcome; the grounds are re-cited and the action is made carryable. 1) CANON FORBIDS (b),
  CITED CORRECTLY THIS TIME. R4 at docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:36-44 makes abandonment
  'gradually and recoverably and never touching the hearth' (:39-40). R5 at :46-51 makes the
  hearth 'the first and smallest thing to cross its own self-sustaining threshold' (:50-51).
  The first consensus cited :63-66 for R5; that range is R7. Option (b) — the hearth as part
  of the live work when the Foundry starts — would make the keel-fire a product of the first
  job, which is R5 reversed. Both parent beats carry it inert: cfd-176:19 'Do not feed the
  hearth', :23 'On the home wait the hearth does not dim'; cfd-183:339 'The hearth does not
  dim.' 2) THE REGISTER THE CORPUS ALREADY USES, ADOPTED. The check is right that the record
  holds the citation for this exact row and it is not the one the ruling reached for:
  cfd-183:243 files 'Bank and hearth as standing scenery' under R8 and R4. A ruling refusing
  (b) should rest on the citation the parent beat already carries rather than mint a new one,
  so R8 and R4 lead and R5 supplies the mechanism. 3) THE BYTES SAY 'SCENERY' IS THE TRUE
  REGISTER, AND ALSO SAY WHY (b) TEMPTS. Re-measured: sit/dawnspur-site/sim.js contains zero
  occurrences of 'hearth' or 'greenhouse'. `#hearth` in sit/dawnspur-site/index.html is a
  span at :235, styled `display:none; pointer-events:none` at :147-152 and revealed by
  `#foundry.live #hearth { display: block; }` at :153. So the span IS lit when the Foundry
  goes live — look, with no state, no hitbox and no write to the heat pin. The skeptic's
  sharpest point stands: the entry's dichotomy is false on the bytes, 'as scenery' and
  'appears with the Foundry' are both true at once. That is why the ruling adds the gloss
  rather than only refusing (b). 4) (d) WOULD PUT A PASSED DESCENDANT UNDER A REFUSED ROW.
  sit/dawnspur-halt/index.html:301 ships `<button type="button" id="terrace"
  aria-label="greenhouse terrace">`, and the signed halt beat writes it at
  cfd-205-halt:105-107: 'Greenhouse terrace. A station that feeds itself. Food already on the
  glass. Blocked: carry / tend / UP — a held island is not a fuel bill.' Striking the site
  row as REFUSED would leave the parent beat forbidding what its passed child ships. 5)
  MOOTNESS, RECORDED NOT RULED. Canon :373 — 'A system is "inherited" only if it **passed a
  sit**' — and the site was sat and not passed (KILLS.md:55-66, 'Sat 2026-08-30, not passed'
  at :58; hub public/index.html:73, 'live — sat 2026-08-30, not passed'). So nothing inherits
  from these rows anyway; what later boards inherit is the passed halt's bytes. True, and
  worth recording, but it is a reason no harm flows either way, not a reason to prefer a
  change. Zero-cost and true beats zero-cost and newly wrong. 6) TWO CHECK FINDINGS REFUTED
  FROM THE RECORD. (i) The check says the sentence 'A system is "inherited" only if it passed
  a sit' begins at canon :372 and that this ruling disagrees with ruling 24. Measured: :371
  ends '...the only new thing on it.', :372 is EMPTY, and :373 is 'A system is "inherited"
  only if it **passed a sit**. A killed system does not'. The first consensus's :373 was
  right; ruling 24's ':372-374' started one line early and is corrected there. (ii) The check
  gives KILLS.md:64-65 for 'Do not recut this path; the pins stand' — that one is correct,
  the first consensus's :63-64 was off, and it is fixed. 7) COST. Price of no change: zero.
  Price of (b): a system row claiming a mechanic the stopped board does not have and can
  never be tested against its pinned bytes (KILLS.md:64-65, 'Do not / recut this path; the
  pins stand'). Price of (d): one supersession now and a contradiction with a passed board.
- *Dissent:* None on the outcome. The live disagreement is about grounds: the skeptic holds
  the question is moot rather than answered — the rows govern nothing because canon :373
  forbids inheriting from a stopped board — and that the orchestrator's stated ground
  ('consistent with cfd-176 and cfd-183') is weaker than the real one. Both readings are
  folded into the rationale; neither changes the ruling. The reader and cost lenses' shared
  worry — that 'as scenery' will be read as 'never drawn' and someone will later refuse a
  hearth glow as decorative motion — is answered by the gloss rather than by a relabel, which
  is the form the canon lens proposed.
- *For David, not blocking:* None. Any change to the site or halt boards' bytes — renaming
  the hearth span, for instance — is a stopped-and-pinned or passed-board edit and was never
  on the table. A refusal of the greenhouse that binds later boards would be a design ruling
  for David, not a register call; the ruling does not make one.
- *Action:* [docs] Add the gloss as a standalone italic paragraph immediately AFTER the 'On
  this sitting' table's last row at cfd-205:224 and before the '---' at :226 — do not insert
