## 23. `cfd-205`'s two R-citations

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: read one table first.)*
In the site beat's Canon check table, is the reason given for Foundry-is-work-one the right
basis, and is the loads-are-one-of-three-claims rule the right rule to cite for the
bill-is-addressed-hauls row?

- *Why:* The site beat's Canon check maps each mechanism to a rulebook rule. The Foundry
  row rests on a prose reason (buildings exist because they do jobs; the Foundry is Heat put
  into ground already reached) rather than a numbered rule, and the bill row cites the rule
  that every load is profit, tending, or buildout.
- *Options:*
  a. **Foundry citation right** — The Foundry row stands on its prose reason as written.
     *Costs:* If it is in fact wrong and left standing, it propagates into every beat that
     inherits the row.
  b. **Foundry citation wrong** — You name the rule or source the row should rest on instead,
     and the row is rewritten to it. *Costs:* None named by the entry.
  c. **Bill row: the three-claims rule is right** — The bill-as-addressed-hauls row keeps its
     citation. *Costs:* If it is in fact wrong and left standing, it propagates into every beat
     that inherits the row.
  d. **Bill row: a different rule** — You name which rule the bill actually rests on, and the
     row is rewritten to it. *Costs:* None named by the entry.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator has no strong read: both are
  rule-mapping judgements inside a table on a stopped board, and the underlying mechanics are
  not in dispute. *Yours:* Which rulebook rule a mechanism rests on is a design judgement;
  the entry offers no strong read and leaves both picks to you.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked. A wrong rule citation propagates into every beat that
  inherits the row.
- *What the entry does not say:* The entry asks whether the Foundry row's rule citation is
  right, but that row cites no numbered rule at all, only a prose reason; the entry does not
  say whether the question is 'is the prose reason sound' or 'which rule should be cited'.

**Question.** Two picks: is the R-citation for *"Foundry is work one"* right, and is R10
the right rule for the bill?

**What the document says.** `docs/cfd-205-beat.md:277`: *"| Foundry is work one | ON |
buildings exist because they do jobs; Foundry is Heat (food into already-reached ground)
|"*. `:280`: *"| Bill = addressed hauls | ON | R10 — a load can be buildout; the loop as
sat is how the haul exists |"*. **The audit cited these as `:268` and `:271`; both have
drifted by seven lines.** Corrected here.

**Blocked.** Nothing.

**Orchestrator's read.** *No strong read.* Both are rule-mapping judgements inside a Canon
check table on a stopped board; the underlying mechanics are not in dispute.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** A wrong rule citation propagates into every beat that
inherits the row.

**RULED IN PART — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09; the rest RETURNED TO
DAVID.** The bill row is right and stands on R10. The Foundry row is wrong as cited — it
cites no rule and no source line — and is rewritten, as an INTERIM under David's reserved
naming, to what the record already holds: R9 for the mechanism, the beat's own 2026-08-30
signature for 'work one', with the existing prose kept as an explicitly unsourced gloss
placed after the table. The choice is unchanged; the adversarial check found three line
cites off by one, a misquoted counsellor, an under-stated reservation and an unplaceable
action, and all four are fixed. Re-measured at e226a9f: the R9 row is at cfd-205:281, not
:282; '§7 KEPT — one new system; SIGNED David 2026-08-30' is at :274, not :273; 'signed
recut 2 — Superheavy; David 2026-08-30' is at :276, not :275 — and :275 is the Foundry row
itself, so the first consensus cited one line as both the defect and its precedent. The two
R1 rows are at :277 and :279. *(majority; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the
skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words.
Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named
under Carried out moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* REVISED after an adversarial check. All four lenses chose (b) and (c); the
  moderation's work was the replacement citation, and the check corrected how that work was
  reported. 1) THE ROW FAILS §6.1's OWN TEST. cfd-205:275 reads '| Foundry is work one | ON |
  buildings exist because they do jobs; Foundry is Heat (food into already-reached ground)
  |'. §6.1's standard is 'cites the rule above (R1-R10) or the source line it rests on'
  (canon :250-251, re-bound by §7's RULED :411-413). This cites neither. Measured: 'because
  they do jobs' / 'buildings exist' returns zero hits across C:/dev/skyrail/docs/lore and
  across this repo outside that cell and the ledger's quotations, and the phrase entered at
  d51b081 (Cursor Agent, 2026-08-30). An uncited mechanism in the one table that exists to
  satisfy §6.1 is the thing §6.1 refuses. 2) THE RECORD ALREADY HOLDS BOTH HALVES. The row
  makes two claims. The mechanism half — 'Foundry is Heat (food into already-reached ground)'
  — is R9's own sentence: 'Heat that warms the ground already reached against Air that opens
  new ground, with no build that does both' (canon :85-86). The same table already cites it
  at :281: 'R9 — Heat ≠ Air; Foundry does Heat, not Air+Heat.' The ordering half — 'work one'
  — is not a rule at all; it is a signed design call, and the beat's face carries it:
  cfd-205:5 'Foundry is work one', :7 '**SIGNED — David, 2026-08-30, word "Signed."**'. The
  table already uses that register twice, at :274 and :276. So the rewrite names nothing new;
  it points at two things already on the page. 3) TWO PROPOSED CITATIONS REFUSED, AND THE
  MISQUOTE THAT CARRIED ONE OF THEM CORRECTED. The check is right and the correction is
  adopted in full. The cost lens's actual risk note reads: 'If the canon-lens counsellor
  reads "Foundry is work one" as an ordering claim (why Foundry before Warehouse) rather than
  a heat claim, R1 alone carries it and R9 is surplus — cheap to trim.' The first consensus
  reported the reverse — that R1 was the trimmable one — and then itself read the row as an
  ordering claim while keeping R9 and dropping R1. The refusal of R1 is still right, on a
  ground the council can actually stand behind rather than on a concession that was never
  made: the row's claims are the Heat lane and the ordering, and the same table already cites
  R1 twice for the marks lane, at :277 ('R1 — marks pay for buildout, they are not heat and
  they are not the bill') and :279 ('R1 — no second currency; no glass wallet'). R9 stays
  because the row makes a mechanism claim as well as an ordering claim; the ordering is
  covered by the signature rather than by a rule, which is what the cost lens's fallback was
  groping for. (ii) World Bible v0.1's resolved cut :421 as the source for 'buildings do
  jobs' — the canon lens's proposal — is refused: entry 17 is open and records that bible
  ruled legacy 33 minutes after cfd-206 merged, and two beats in this very lineage instruct
  'World Bible v0.1 is LEGACY except §8 (sections 2, 4, 6). Do not cite World Bible §12 as
  live canon' (cfd-207:86-87 — not :83-85, corrected — and cfd-208:105-107). Resting a new
  citation on a bible whose status is an unruled entry decides entry 17 sideways. Prefer null
  to a plausible source. 4) CITING R9 HERE DOES NOT BRUSH ENTRY 12. Entry 12 is about the
  heat board's WARM on cfd-176 (canon :105 versus :117-118). The site's Foundry does Heat
  only, on reached ground, with the rim refusing — and the adjacent row at :281 already cites
  R9 for it. No side is taken. 5) THE BILL ROW IS RIGHT, AND THE ALTERNATIVE IS WRONG IN THE
  BEAT'S OWN TERMS. R10 at canon :89-97: a load 'can be buildout, materials and heat carried
  out to extend the network' (:93-94). An addressed haul landing at the site to fill its bill
  is that claim. R1 would be the wrong rule: the beat's hardest kill is 'Bill payable in
  marks alone (killed hardest)' (cfd-205:232-233, with the prose at :131-132, 'There is no
  marks-to-bill path'), so the marks rule is the one the bill refuses, not the one it rests
  on. 6) PROPAGATION, MEASURED. The entry's cost line — 'propagates into every beat that
  inherits the row' — is zero today: the downstream rows carry it as 'inherited' with no
  R-cite of their own (cfd-205-halt:207-208, cfd-206:174, cfd-207:198, cfd-208:227). Nothing
  has propagated. That lowers the urgency; it does not change the answer.
- *Dissent:* The reader lens's fallback, strengthened by the adversarial check and now
  partly adopted: option (b) is worded 'You name the rule', which reserves the naming to
  David, so the row should not be quietly re-based without asking. The first consensus
  reported 'None blocking' and asked only whether the prose is his line; that under-stated
  what the entry reserves, and the ruling is now explicitly INTERIM with the reserved
  question put to him in full. Its stronger form — leave the row at (a) and merely flag it
  'uncited under §6.1' — still loses, because the rewrite names nothing new: R9's sentence is
  already inside the row's own prose and already cited in the same table at :281, and the
  signature is already on the beat's first page at :7. Pointing at two things the record
  already holds is applying, not designing, and it leaves David free to substitute a
  different rule at the cost of one cell. The canon lens's World Bible citation and the cost
  lens's R1 are the two substantive dissents on content and are answered at (3); the cost
  lens's position is quoted correctly there after the first consensus reversed it.
- *Requires David:* The entry reserves this, so it is asked rather than assumed. Option (b)
  is worded 'You name the rule or source the row should rest on instead' — the naming is

## 24. `cfd-205-halt`'s "inherited" cells for systems that never passed

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a yes/no.)*
In the halt-walk beat's systems table, do the rows for Works, SITE, LAND and CAST get
relabelled from inherited to kept by signature?

- *Why:* Those systems came from the site board, which was sat and stopped and never
  passed. In this corpus inherited means carried from a passed board, so the label claims a
  lineage the site board never earned; what actually happened is that you signed to keep them
  over a board that failed.
- *Options:*
  a. **Yes, rename** — The cells read kept by signature, recording that these systems survive
     because you kept them, not because a board passed. *Costs:* A small edit to the halt-walk
     beat's table.
  b. **No, keep inherited** — The cells stay as written. *Costs:* The corpus loses the
     distinction between what passed and what was kept anyway.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator reads it as rename them:
  kept-by-signature is a different and more interesting fact than inherited, and the current
  word claims a pass that did not happen. *Yours:* Whether your signature over a failed board
  counts as inheritance is a naming call only you can make, since the signature was yours.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked; the halt-walk beat keeps a label that says a lineage
  came from a board that never passed.

**Question.** Yes or no: rename the cell from *"inherited"* to *"kept by signature"* for
Works/SITE/LAND/CAST?

**What the document says.** `docs/cfd-205-halt-beat.md:236` *"| **Works — SITE, address,
bill** | **ON** — Foundry is work one | inherited; SITE/LAND/CAST sim kept |"*, `:238`
*"| **LAND as arrival, not SEND** | **ON** | inherited |"*, `:239` *"| **CAST = OPEN: bill
full + terrace food → Foundry, food in, Heat step** | **ON** | inherited Works |"*. **The
audit cited `:203-206`, which at HEAD is the table header and the first two rows.**
Corrected here: the rows are `:236`, `:238`, `:239`.

**What source says.** Those systems came from `/dawnspur-site/`, which was **sat and not
passed** (KILLS.md's 2026-08-30 Dawnspur Site entry; hub `public/index.html:67`, *"live — sat 2026-08-30, not
passed"*), so "inherited" claims a lineage from a board that never earned one.

**Blocked.** Nothing.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Rename them.* "Inherited" in this corpus means "carried from a
passed board"; these were kept by your signature over a board that failed, which is a
different and more interesting fact.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** The corpus loses the distinction between what passed and what
was kept anyway.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** Rename. cfd-205-halt's 'On
this sitting' table stops using 'inherited' for systems that never passed a sit. Five cells
carry the claim, not the three the entry lists — :206 'inherited Home', :207 'inherited;
SITE/LAND/CAST sim kept', :208 'inherited', :209 'inherited', :210 'inherited Works' — and
all five become 'kept by signature', naming the signature and the failed sit they came
from. The cells cite the existing 2026-08-30 signature; they sign nothing and move no pin.
The choice and the scope are unchanged; the adversarial check found one cite stale in a
docket whose whole method is correcting stale cites, and it is fixed: the site's hub tag is
at public/index.html:73, 'live — sat 2026-08-30, not passed'; :67 is the HALT's card, 'live
— passed its sit'. The first consensus inherited :67 from the entry without re-measuring,
and it was the cite that convicted the word. *(majority; four lenses — canon, cost, the
reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not
David's words. Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the
landing named under Carried out moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* REVISED after an adversarial check. Unanimous on renaming; the split was scope —
  three cells (reader, canon), four (cost), five (skeptic). Five is right, and the table
  itself is the argument. 1) CANON DEFINES THE WORD, AND THE DEFINITION IS RULED.
  docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:373-375, inside §7 whose RULED marker is at :265 ('**RULED —
  David, 2026-08-26.**'): 'A system is "inherited" only if it **passed a sit**. A killed

  Only the halt beat's own pre-sit table claims the site and the failed Home cut. Downstream

## 25. `cfd-205-halt:92-93` — a sit finding, or a ruling?

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a yes/no.)*
Does the sentence in the halt board's Come-home beat saying that buildings, feed, lamp,
Foundry and Home writing landed but the walk did not get relabelled as your ruling rather
than presented as something the sit found?

- *Why:* Your verbatim report from that sit was that you lit the lamp and started the
  foundry; it says nothing about the walk. The record's own review reads the
  walk-did-not-land sentence as a ruling, not a finding, but the beat presents it in the same
  breath as the sit's findings.
- *Options:*
  a. **Yes, label it a ruling** — The beat says plainly that the walk-did-not-land sentence
     is your conclusion, separate from what you did in the sit. *Costs:* None named by the
     entry.
  b. **No, leave it** — The sentence stays reading as a sit finding. *Costs:* A ruling reads
     as an observation and gets re-litigated.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator reads it as say so: separating
  what the player did from what you concluded is the distinction this project keeps losing,
  and your verbatim is right there to separate them. *Yours:* Whether that sentence is your
  ruling is something only you can confirm, since you are the one who concluded it.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked; the sentence stays open to being reargued as if it
  were an observation.

**Question.** Yes or no: relabel it as a ruling?

**What the document says.** `docs/cfd-205-halt-beat.md:92-93`: *"Buildings-as-buildings,
feed, lamp and Foundry as actions, and Home writing landed. The walk did not. Recut so
only one action is live at a"*.

**What source says.** The audit's disposition, `docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:1264-1265`:
*"'Home writing landed. The walk did not.' as a sit finding (it is a ruling; say so)"*.
KILLS.md's 2026-08-30 Dawnspur Halt Home-sit entry records the sit verbatim as *"Lit the lamp and started the foundry."* —
the walk sentence is not in it.

**Blocked.** Nothing.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Say so.* The distinction between what the player did and what you
concluded is the one this project keeps losing, and the verbatim is right there to
separate them.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** A ruling reads as an observation and gets re-litigated.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** Label it. The paragraph at
cfd-205-halt:65-69 gets a dated parenthetical marking it as a ruling and not the sit's
words — attributed to the recut's reading carried by the 2026-08-30 signature, never to
David as his own sentence. The sit's verbatim is the one line above it, and the label says
so. The choice is unchanged; the adversarial check found the action contradicting the
ruling's own adopted constraint, and three line cites off by one. The paragraph runs
:65-69, ending 'Do not recut into louder Home slogans.' Appending after :66 would land the
label mid-paragraph, between 'Recut so only one action is live at a time.' and 'Dead jobs
remain tappable buttons...' — exactly the placement the adopted caution forbids, since
'Home writing landed' is no more in the verbatim than 'the walk did not' is. The
parenthetical goes after :69. *(unanimous; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the
skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words.
Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named
under Carried out moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* REVISED after an adversarial check. Unanimous on labelling, and unanimous —
  against the entry's own wording — on whose ruling it is. The check's placement finding is
  adopted; two of its three cite corrections are adopted and one is refuted from the record.
  1) THE VERBATIM IS ONE SENTENCE, AND ITS CITE IS CORRECTED. cfd-205-halt:60-61: 'Sit
  closed, not a pass. Verbatim: "Lit the lamp and started the foundry." He did the two
  opening can-dos and stopped.' The first consensus gave :59-60; :59 is the recut's hashes
  line, 'Live `/dawnspur-halt/` Home recut `c1b66ee5`, index `7aa764fa`, sim'. KILLS.md:41-42
  records the same one line. Canon §7.4's evidence reads the sit the same way. 2) THE
  SENTENCE CANNOT BE A SIT FINDING ON ITS FACE. cfd-205-halt:65-66 reads
  'Buildings-as-buildings, feed, lamp and Foundry as actions, and Home writing landed. The
  walk did not.' The walk is this recut's one NEW system — the beat's own header at :7, 'with
  the walk. One NEW system: the walk. One live can-do at a time.' — so it was not on the
  board that was sat. A sit cannot find that a system which was not on the board did not
  land. It is a diagnosis that produced the recut, printed under a heading called 'The sit
  that failed'. (The check places that header text at :6; measured, :6 is the SIGNED line,
  '**SIGNED — David, 2026-08-30, Superheavy named it.** Recut `/dawnspur-halt/`', and the
  NEW-system text is at :7. The first consensus's :7 stands; the check is refuted here. The
  SIGNED line itself is at :6, which BOTH the first consensus and the check gave as :5 —
  corrected.) 3) AUTHORSHIP, MEASURED. `git log -S'The walk did not.' --
  docs/cfd-205-halt-beat.md` returns 923002d, 'CFD-205: recut /dawnspur-halt/ — Come home
  (the walk)', Cursor Agent <cursoragent@cursor.com>, Sun Aug 30 21:09:47 2026 — the same

  adopted by signature the same day. Canon §7.4:712-716 records the same division of labour
  ('The PM then refused the obvious next move by name'). 4) THE ENTRY'S OPTION (a) IS WORDED
  WRONG AND ALL FOUR LENSES SAID SO INDEPENDENTLY. The entry offers 'label it as your
  conclusion'. The record shows David signing the beat that contains the sentence, not
  writing it. Labelling it 'RULED — David' or 'David's finding' would put a conclusion in his
  mouth that the record does not contain — precisely the failure the ledger's
  never-in-David's-name rule exists to prevent, and the reason the label rests on the
  signature it can see. His own words on the walk are null in this repository; prefer null to
  a plausible attribution. 5) WHY IT MATTERS TO THE NEXT READER. The next recut author works
  under §7.4 and reads that section as the evidence. Left unlabelled, 'the walk did not'
  reads as a sit result for a system never played — the manufactured result §7.5 exists to
  prevent. KILLS.md already keeps the two apart ('Two opening can-dos; he stopped... Recut:
  the walk'); the beat runs them together under one heading. 6) COST, RE-MEASURED.
  test/dawnspur-halt.test.js:192 and :194 assert /the walk/ and /Lit the lamp and started the

- *Dissent:* None on the outcome; the four lenses converged, including on refusing the
  entry's own wording. The reader lens's caution is adopted rather than rejected, and this

  'Home writing landed' is no more in the verbatim than 'the walk did not' is — so the

  that would overturn the ruling is a David verbatim from Linear CFD-205 naming the walk —
  unreachable from the repo, so recorded as null rather than assumed either way.
- *For David, not blocking:* None blocking. If the sentence is your own — a verbatim on
  Linear CFD-205 that this repository cannot reach — say so and the label simplifies to
  'David's ruling, 2026-08-30'. The council will not assume it in either direction.

## 26. `cfd-209`'s appended pre-registration note — does it read as yours?

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a yes/no.)*
Does the dated note appended under the dice beat's pre-registered null sentence stand as
written, or do you strike it?

- *Why:* Before the sit, the beat pre-registered a null outcome — if you send once from the
  free Halt and stop, the places are still scenery and the desk was doing the work all along
  — but the free Halt is the line board's route, and on the dice board the Halt is Home, so
  the null as written describes a send the board cannot make. After the sit the write-up
  wanted to reword that sentence; the orchestrator refused, on the reading that the
  rulebook's pre-registration rule exists so that sentence is never rewritten after the sit
  it predicted, left it verbatim, and appended a dated note beneath it explaining the naming
  collision (which the Two-ways-from-here board found one beat downstream) and recording that
  the sit met a failed run, so the null did not fire. The note is in the beat in your voice,
  and you did not write it.
- *Options:*
  a. **Leave the note** — The pre-registered sentence stays verbatim and the dated note
     beneath it carries the correction. *Costs:* A note in your voice that you did not write
     sits under a ruled pre-registration.
  b. **Strike the note** — The note is removed; the pre-registered sentence stays verbatim
     with no explanation of the collision beside it. *Costs:* None named by the entry.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator reads it as leave it: refusing
  to rewrite the pre-registration was right, and the note carries the correction without
  touching the sentence. *Yours:* Whether a note in your voice that you did not write may
  stand is yours; the record explicitly reserves that you may strike it.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked; the note is already landed. It stays in your voice
  until you say otherwise.

**Question.** Yes or no: does the note stand as written, or do you strike it?

**What the document says.** `docs/cfd-209-beat.md:423` (the audit cited `:396`; **drifted by
27 lines**, corrected here) — a dated parenthetical opening *"(Recorded 2026-09-01, after
the sit, and the sentence above is left as it was pre-registered: 'the free Halt' is the
line board's route …"* and closing *"This is the name collision the cfd-210 re-cut found
one beat downstream, recorded here at its upstream.)"* The pre-registered sentence it sits
under is at `:395`: *"The run came home short and the larder covered it."*

**What source says.** `docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:1236-1239`: *"the synthesis
re-worded the pre-registered null; the orchestrator refused that form and appended a dated
note beneath the sentence kept verbatim, because a pre-registration is the one sentence a
beat must not rewrite after the sit it pre-registered (canon §7.5's whole point); **David
may strike the note.**"*

**Blocked.** Nothing; it is already landed at `5ad4c6f`.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Leave it.* The refusal to rewrite the pre-registration was
right — it is exactly what §7.5 protects — and the note carries the correction without
touching the sentence.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** A note in your voice that you did not write, on a ruled
pre-registration.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** Leave the note, as the interim
ruling. The strike is reserved to David by name in two places in the record and stays his.
The pre-registered sentence at cfd-209:419-421 stands verbatim, the dated note beneath it
at :423 stands, and the entry's premise that the note reads 'in your voice' is corrected:
it opens by dating and disowning itself, after both the signature and the pass. Unchanged
after the adversarial check, which found no material defect and re-verified every fact; one
nit is fixed — the signed block runs from its heading at :39 to the '---' at :77, not to
:75, and it is used only to establish that :423 sits outside it, which is true either way.
*(unanimous; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one
adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in this block are at
`e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named under Carried out moved some of
them.)*

- *Why:* Unanimous, and the reasons stack rather than compete. Every fact was re-opened at
  e226a9f for this revision and held. 1) THE NOTE IS THE FORM CANON REQUIRES. §7.5 (heading
  docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:820, 'RULED, David, 2026-09-01') protects the
  pre-registration because the value is the timing, not the writing; rewriting the sentence
  after the sit it predicted is the thing the rule exists to stop. The note leaves :419-421
  byte-for-byte and appends. §6.4 (canon :257-258): 'Supersession stays by edit: when a rule
  here is refined, this file changes in place with the date and the reason, never a second
  file.' §7.7 (heading :1029, RULED 2026-09-04) puts a board's status in the beat, superseded
  in place. The note at :423 is dated, reasoned and in place — the same form landed 25 times
  in 5ad4c6f. 2) THE 'IN YOUR VOICE' PREMISE DOES NOT HOLD. Read at HEAD, :423 opens
  '*(Recorded 2026-09-01, after the sit, and the sentence above is left as it was
  pre-registered:' — third person, dated after both the signature (cfd-209:39, '## SIGNED —
  David, 2026-08-31.', with his word at :41, 'signed') and the pass, sitting outside the
  signed block (heading :39, closing rule :77), and 5ad4c6f's body records the orchestrator
  appending it. David's voice appears in that beat only as quotation. 3) ITS FACTS HOLD AT
  HEAD. The note says the null as pre-registered describes a send the board cannot make:
  sit/dice-at-the-places/sim.js's canSend returns false unless `s.consistAt === 'halt'`, so
  the Halt is home and not a destination, and the note records that 'The sit met a failed
  run, so the null did not fire' — it governs no result and reinterprets no outcome. 4)
  STRIKING HAS TWO COSTS THE ENTRY RECORDS AS 'NONE NAMED', BOTH MEASURED. First, the beat
  would keep a live sentence about a send the board cannot make, with nothing beside it
  saying so — and the sentence itself may not be rewritten, by §7.5. That republishes the
  desk-to-city 'free Halt' collision inside a passed beat, at the exact upstream where
  cfd-210's withdrawn 'free 68 for 10' fork found it one beat later. Second,
  test/beats-lexicon-ledger.js:371 narrates the disposition as a pinned string that includes
  'David may strike the note.' — so a strike must edit the ledger in the same commit or the
  ledger describes a note that no longer exists. 5) THE HONEST RESIDUAL, recorded rather than
  fixed: the note's re-reading of the null ('read the null as "sends Mosswake once, brings
  her home, and stops"') is a post-sit gloss on a pre-registration, in tension with §7.5's
  spirit even though the sentence itself is untouched and the null never fired. A future
  author must not cite it as licence to gloss other nulls after their sits. That belongs in
  the ledger, not in a strike.
- *Dissent:* None on leaving. The live disagreement is about whose act a strike is: the
  skeptic and the cost lens hold that option (b) is not the council's to take at all, because
  docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:1295 and test/beats-lexicon-ledger.js:371 both
  reserve it to David by name. That reading is adopted — hence 'interim ruling', and hence
  the reservation is restated rather than closed. The cost lens's flagged alternative — one
  clause of attribution inside the note ('recorded by the orchestrator') — is not taken,
  because the entry did not put it on the table; it is surfaced to David instead.
- *For David, not blocking:* The strike is reserved to you by name
  (docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:1295, 'David may strike the note.';
  test/beats-lexicon-ledger.js:371). The note stays until you say otherwise, and this ruling
  does not extinguish that. Two things worth your word: (1) if what bothers you is that it
  reads as signed text, the smaller fix the entry did not offer is one clause of attribution
  inside the note — 'recorded by the orchestrator, 2026-09-01' — which is one edit; (2) if
  you strike it, the pinned ledger string at test/beats-lexicon-ledger.js:371 must be edited
  in the same commit or it describes a note that no longer exists.
- *Action:* [record] Two corrections in the ledger: the note is at cfd-209:423 beneath the
  pre-registered null at :419-421, not at :395 (which falls inside '## Wanted after one sit',
  heading :390); and the entry's 'in your voice' premise does not hold — the note is a dated
  third-person parenthetical outside the signed block (heading :39, closing rule :77),
  appended by the orchestrator per 5ad4c6f. Also record the residual: the note's post-sit
  re-reading of the null is not precedent for glossing any other null after its sit.

## 27. The desk PASSED markers in the inherited-board tables

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a yes/no.)*
In the inherited-board tables of the dice beat and the Two-ways-from-here beat, do the two
desk boards (the line board and the dispatch board) keep their PASSED markers, or are both
struck?

- *Why:* Earlier cuts of those tables marked only the four city passes; the current cuts
  also mark the two desk boards as PASSED, each with a note beneath explaining that the
  passes were real and dated. The record's own instruction is that the two tables must match:
  strike the desk markers from both or keep them in both.
- *Options:*
  a. **Keep both** — Both beats continue to list the line and dispatch boards as passed
     inheritances, with the explanatory notes. *Costs:* If the desk passes do not in fact
     support the rows, two city beats claim a desk inheritance they cannot support.
  b. **Strike both** — Both beats drop the desk PASSED markers and list only the four city
     passes. *Costs:* If the desk passes do support the rows, two city beats drop an inheritance
     they can support.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* The orchestrator reads it as keep both: the
  tables claim to list what the board inherits, the rows these beats inherit under the
  rulebook's cumulative-board rule genuinely require those two desk passes, and the notes
  beneath already say so. *Yours:* Strike-or-keep is yours; the entry gives its read and
  stops there, and the underlying call is whether two city beats should show a desk
  inheritance on their face.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked. The risk is two city beats claiming a desk
  inheritance they cannot support, or dropping one they can.

**Question.** Yes or no: strike them from both tables, or keep both?

**What the document says.** `docs/cfd-209-beat.md:104-105` and `docs/cfd-210-beat.md:85-86`
(the audit cited `cfd-210:80-81`; **drifted by five lines**, corrected here) both carry
*"| `/dawnspur-line/` **PASSED** | `18b1324f` | `b6f21db0` |"* and *"|
`/dawnspur-dispatch/` **PASSED** | `576ce2b6` | `31aead60` |"*, each table followed by a
parenthetical explaining that earlier cuts marked only the four city passes.

**What source says.** The audit's own instruction: *"the desk PASSED markers (landed in
both tables; strike both or neither)"*
(`docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:1267-1269`).

**Blocked.** Nothing.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Keep both.* The tables claim to list what the board inherits, and
the §7 rows genuinely require those two passes — the parentheticals at `:107-112` and
`:88` already say so with the shas re-derived.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** Two city beats claim a desk inheritance they cannot support,
or drop one they can.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** Keep both. The two desk PASSED
markers stay in both Seat tables, because rows in both beats inherit from those boards and
canon requires an inherited system to be cited to the beat that passed it. The passes are
real and the pins verify at HEAD. Separately, cfd-209's Seat parenthetical cites the wrong
line for both passes and is re-pointed. The choice is unchanged; the adversarial check
found this ruling self-refuting on its own canon cites — a ruling whose finding is that a
parenthetical citing the wrong line is a defect worth fixing, carrying three wrong cites of
its own — and it is right. Re-measured: 'A new sitting **inherits what has already passed**
and adds one thing' is at docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:369-370, not :366; 'An inherited
system is cited to the beat that passed it' is at :412-413, inside §7's 'What does NOT
change', not :408-409; and 'A system is "inherited" only if it **passed a sit**' is at :373
— the first consensus gave :372 and the check accepted it, but :372 is an empty line.
*(unanimous; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one
adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in this block are at
`e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named under Carried out moved some of
them.)*

- *Why:* REVISED after an adversarial check. Unanimous, on measurement rather than
  preference — and the moderation found one real defect underneath the question. The check
  confirmed everything except the ruling's own canon cites, which it is right about, and
  those are corrected here. 1) CANON MAKES THE MARKERS LOAD-BEARING, RE-CITED. §7, whose
  RULED marker is at :265 ('**RULED — David, 2026-08-26.**'): at :369-370, 'A new sitting
  **inherits what has already passed** and adds one thing'; at :373, 'A system is "inherited"

  3588bb4:sit/dawnspur-line/sim.js | sha256sum` gives 18b1324f and HEAD's copy is
  byte-identical; `git show 663d4fa:sit/dawnspur-dispatch/sim.js | sha256sum` gives 576ce2b6
  and HEAD's copy is identical. Those are the pins the tables print at cfd-209:104-105 and

  adversarial check independently re-derived both. 3) THE BLANK CELL IS THE THING THAT
  ALREADY FAILED ONCE. 5ad4c6f records cfd-210 having claimed '/dawnspur-line/ is not marked
  PASSED ... the route could not have been inherited', withdrawn when the pass was found at
  cfd-201:92, cfd-203 and canon §7.1 — 'The Seat table's blank cell was the stale thing,

  `:1549` — and `/dawnspur-dispatch/` PASSED 2026-08-26 at host `663d4fa` —
  `docs/cfd-196-beat.md:835-838`'. Measured: cfd-203:1511 is analysis prose mid-sentence
  ('because the stop requires it rather than because it pays. §7.2 still passes either'); the

  2026-08-28**.', and David's verdict at :1551, '**David's verdict, 2026-08-28, in full:
  _"passes"_.**'. So only :1511 is wrong — :1549 was already right, and the check's proposed

  about the card list and the slider; cfd-196's only PASSED line is :852, '# The sit —

  'Seat-table scope — city-scoped by intent or not' item at :1290-1291 is genuinely open.

  wants city-scoped Seats, both markers and both parentheticals come out together, and the
  desk provenance moves into the From column of the rows that need it.
- *Dissent:* None. The nearest counter is the reader lens's own risk note — that a desk

  board — which cfd-210:430-440 already refutes in prose and which the two parentheticals

  not for striking the markers. The adversarial check's one substantive amendment — replacing
  the cfd-203 cite with ':1548-1550' — is refused on measurement: :1549 in the existing
  parenthetical is already correct, only :1511 is wrong, and the repair is the narrower

- *For David, not blocking:* None blocking. One register question if you want it settled

## 28. `cfd-196`'s three register deviations

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a yes/no.)*
Do you accept, as the settled record of the dispatch board, the three places where its
end-of-run sentences departed from the example sentences you signed: (1) neither closing
sentence blames the weather ("lost on the way" rather than "lost to the weather on the
way"), because on this board only one route's failures are weather; (2) the paid closing
sentence carries a fourth number, the turned-back count, beside runs out, cargoes banked
and stakes lost; (3) the turned-back sentence names whoever actually rode, never "the
crew"?

- *Why:* When the board was built its closing sentences came out different from the signed
  examples, and the beat records the three differences openly rather than hiding them. The
  board passed on 2026-08-26 with those sentences, so the question is whether the beat's
  record of the departures is accepted as the passed board's settled wording.
- *Options:*
  a. **Accept** — The beat's record of the three departures becomes the passed board's
     register; the signed example stays as the history it departed from. *Costs:* Nothing
     further to build; the board passed with these sentences.
  b. **Reverse** — The departures are not accepted; the beat says they are yours to accept or
     reverse, and reversing means the board's sentences go back to the signed wording. *Costs:*
     The entry does not price a reversal. Its one stated cost, either way, is a passed board
     whose record disagrees with the board.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* Orchestrator's read: accept them — the board
  passed with those sentences, and a record that lists its departures against the signed
  example is the right shape for a passed board. *Yours:* Whether the departures are accepted
  or reversed: the beat itself says these are David's to accept or reverse at the sit.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked. What stays wrong is that a passed board's record
  disagrees with the board.
- *What the entry does not say:* The entry names the third departure as "the registers now
  key on cost rather than the implementer's first test", but the beat at HEAD counts its own
  three as items 1, 2 and 4 — weather, the fourth figure, and the turned-back sentence naming
  whoever rode — with the cost keying folded into item 2 as its cause. This card carries the
  beat's three.

**Question.** Yes or no: are the three deviations accepted as the passed record's register?

**What the document says.** `docs/cfd-196-beat.md:23-42` records them itself: *"1. **Neither
terminal register names the weather.**"*; *"2. **The paid register carries a fourth figure
— the turned-back count.**"*; and the third, *"the registers now key on"* cost rather than
the implementer's first test.

**What source says.** `docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:985-986` records the third
as **built** — *"cfd-196's third register deviation and the roster write's dating
(`ed7f49d` → `2a3e9dd`)"* — leaving the acceptance question for the pair.

**Blocked.** Nothing; the board passed 2026-08-26.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Accept them.* The board passed with those registers; the beat now
records the deviations against the signed example rather than hiding them, which is the
correct shape for a passed record.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** A passed board's record disagrees with the board.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** The three register deviations
recorded in docs/cfd-196-beat.md are ACCEPTED as the passed dispatch board's settled
register. The acceptance is recorded against the beat's own items 1, 2 and 4 — no weather
clause in either terminal register; the paid register's fourth figure (the turned-back
count); the turned-back sentence naming whoever rode rather than 'the crew' — and NOT
against the decisions entry's paraphrase 'the registers now key on cost', which is item 2's
stated cause, not a third deviation. Item 3 of the beat is the document going stale and is
not a deviation. By counsel on David's instruction, 2026-09-09; not in David's name.
*(unanimous; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one
adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in this block are at
`e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named under Carried out moved some of
them.)*

- *Why:* UNCHANGED on the check; three line pointers re-derived at e226a9f and corrected
  here. All four counsellors chose accept, and the record carries it on three independent
  grounds. (1) David's signature on the beat is 'SIGNED — David, 2026-08-26: "signed — go,
  and rule the seam my way."' at docs/cfd-196-beat.md:11-13, and departure 1 is that ruling's
  direct consequence: the seam ruling makes the Core Line's failure agent the line, not
  weather, and only Cloud Basin's per-run agent is weather, so a terminal blaming weather
  'would misread its own record, which this beat kills' — item 1 runs :23-30 and that clause
  is at :28-29 (the earlier cite of :17-23 was drift and is superseded). (2) Where a signed
  EXAMPLE and a signed RULE diverge, the discipline is to apply the rule and record the
  departure. The rule is 'every terminal state says what happened, in the board's words'
  (docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:156-157, section 4 item 5, carried by the SIGNED section
  5). Item 2's fourth figure exists because keying on cost let the clean register fire over
  thirteen runs out against four banked with nine turned back, so the count is what
  reconciles the gap in words instead of leaving the reader to subtract (beat :29-42). Item 4
  exists because the opening roster is 0, so the signed wording would have promised a crew
  home that never rode (beat :50-60) — a terminal saying what did not happen. (3) Verified at
  HEAD: public/index.html:91 marks the board 'live — passed its sit 2026-08-26' and publishes
  per-file shas at :93; the shipped sentences are pinned by
  test/dawnspur-dispatch.test.js:1374 (the clean register, inside the endSentence assert at
  :1373-1375), :1377 (the run sentence) and test/dawnspur-line.test.js:1056 (inside the
  assert at :1054-1058), and no test grades the signed EXAMPLE's wording — 'lost to the
  weather on the way' occurs only at docs/cfd-196-beat.md:24 and :263 and in the decisions
  document. Acceptance therefore changes no byte and costs nothing.
- *Dissent:* The strongest reservation, from the canon lens, is that accepting ratifies a
  paid register carrying a fourth figure where the beat's own Ends section specified three,
  so 'the register is three numbers' stops being a discipline a later beat can lean on. It
  did not change the choice because the beat states the reconciling reason in terms, and the
  correct remedy is to restate the discipline going forward as 'the register reconciles its
  own arithmetic in words' rather than to reverse a passed board. The reader lens's second
  risk is carried with it and was omitted from the first draft of this dissent: accepting
  item 2 sets a precedent that a terminal register may carry a fourth figure, and a later
  board may copy the shape without the arithmetic-gap reason that earned it here — so the
  ledger row must record the REASON, not just the acceptance. The skeptic's second
  reservation — that acceptance is close to vacuous since the board already shipped — is
  answered by the ledger's purpose: the row exists to record that the departures were read,
  not discovered.
- *For David, not blocking:* Only if he wants option b. Reversal edits the shipped,
  hash-pinned bytes of a board marked 'live — passed its sit 2026-08-26' and re-cuts three
  test pins, which the lineage lock (docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:406-409) and the standing
  rules put outside the council. Exact question: 'Do you want the dispatch board's three
  end-of-run sentences returned to the signed example wording — including restoring the
  crew-home clause at roster 0 — accepting a re-cut and re-pin of sit/dawnspur-dispatch plus
  the three test assertions that freeze the shipped words
  (test/dawnspur-dispatch.test.js:1374, :1377 and test/dawnspur-line.test.js:1056)?' The
  interim ruling that stays within the rules is a.
- *Action:* [record] Write the ruling into the decisions ledger as RULED BY COUNSEL ON
  DAVID'S INSTRUCTION, 2026-09-09 — never in David's name — naming the beat's items 1, 2 and
  4 as what was accepted, and recording the REASON item 2's fourth figure was earned (it
  reconciles the arithmetic gap in words) so a later beat cannot cite this acceptance as
  licence for a fourth figure generally.
  (C:/dev/skyrail-boards/docs/decisions-open-2026-09-02.md) · [docs] Supersede in place,
  inside entry 28, the sentence naming the third departure as 'the registers now key on cost
  rather than the implementer's first test'. Verified at HEAD: docs/cfd-196-beat.md:15-19
  counts the three deviations as items 1, 2 and 4 and calls item 3 'this document going
  stale'; the cost keying is stated inside item 2 (:29-35) as its cause. The entry's own
  'What the entry does not say' block already flags this and should be folded into the
  corrected sentence rather than left as a second reading.
  (C:/dev/skyrail-boards/docs/decisions-open-2026-09-02.md)

## 29. `cfd-176:19` and `:41` — the two ellipsis readings

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: pick one of two or three.)*
Two picks on the heat board's beat: should the sentence "GOODS / B / HOLD are not on the
same row" be corrected, and should "dest A's core does not die unless fed" be given a
qualifier saying when it holds?

- *Why:* Both are readings of a short sentence, not measured errors — the board's facts are
  confirmed either way. The one measured caution is that the button row on the board actually
  holds four buttons (LEAVE is on it too), so a correction that names only GOODS / B / HOLD
  would itself need correcting the first time someone counted the row.
- *Options:*
  a. **Sentence 1 — correct it** — Rewrite the row sentence to match the board. *Costs:* A
     rewrite naming only GOODS / B / HOLD would need correcting again the first time someone
     counted the row, because LEAVE is on it too.
  b. **Sentence 1 — leave it** — Keep the sentence as it stands, read as the author's
     shorthand. *Costs:* None named by the entry; the board's facts are confirmed on either
     reading.
  c. **Sentence 2 — scope it** — Add a qualifier saying under what conditions the core does
     not die unless fed. *Costs:* An edit to a passed board's beat; the entry does not say what
     the qualifier would be, and its one priced risk is a correction that itself needs
     correcting.
  d. **Sentence 2 — leave it** — Keep the sentence unscoped, read as the author's shorthand.
     *Costs:* None named by the entry; the board's facts are confirmed on either reading.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* Orchestrator's read: leave both — the board
  facts hold on either reading, the sweep's own caution shows the fix introduces a new error,
  and this is the same kind of shorthand-reading question you already ruled once was yours to
  make. *Yours:* Both readings — the sweep marked both as David's to decide, not the sweep's
  to assume.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked. The risk is a correction that itself needs
  correcting.

**Question.** Two picks: does *"GOODS / B / HOLD are not on the same row"* get corrected,
and does *"does not die unless fed"* get scoped?

**What source says.** `docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:957-964`: *"Sweeper SUPERSEDE, refuter DAVID
on both. Both are ellipsis/scope readings, not measured errors: the board facts are

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** LEAVE BOTH. cfd-176-beat.md:19
'GOODS / B / HOLD are not on the same row' is not corrected, and :41 'dest A's core does

- *Why:* UNCHANGED — the adversarial check found no factual or authority defect and

## 30. `wait` — MEDIUM or HIGH?

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: pick one of two or three.)*
Every board has a function called wait; on eleven boards it does nothing, on the heat board
it marks the greenhouse step gone and fires by itself 1.5 seconds after the train is home —
is that a MEDIUM problem or a HIGH one?

- *Why:* The audit first called it HIGH, partly on the claim that it ends the sitting; the
  reviewer found that claim wrong (the WARM step already ends the sitting, and the timer's
  only visible effect is the greenhouse sprite fading, which the board documents as its
  design). The reviewer still allowed HIGH is defensible because heat's bytes are frozen by
  twelve test files, so the difference can never be brought into line, only renamed on the
  other eleven.
- *Options:*
  a. **MEDIUM** — It does not end the sitting and the timer's effect is the documented
     design. *Costs:* The label understates how hard it is to ever fix — twelve frozen files.
  b. **HIGH** — The freeze count dominates: the divergence can never be conformed, only
     renamed elsewhere. *Costs:* The label rests on how hard it is to fix, not on how much harm
     it does.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* Orchestrator's read: MEDIUM — the sentence HIGH
  rested on was shown wrong, and the freeze-count argument is about difficulty, not harm.
  *Yours:* The label only. The entry says the guard pins the behaviour regardless of which
  word is chosen.
- *If undecided:* Nothing — explicitly. The guard already pins the mechanism either way;
  the entry calls this the cheapest call on the list.

**Question.** Pick one severity.

**What the document says.** `docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:838-843` *(an earlier cut cited `:806-810`, which is the guard’s own description and never held these words)*: *"The reviewer
reads MEDIUM on the report's own grounds (it does not end the sitting; the timer's effect
is the documented design) and says HIGH is defensible if the pin count dominates (twelve
test files freeze heat's bytes, so the divergence can never be conformed, only renamed on
the other eleven). Both readings are recorded; the guard pins the mechanism regardless of
the label."*

**Blocked.** Nothing — explicitly. The guard pins the mechanism either way.

**Orchestrator's read.** *MEDIUM.* The reviewer refuted the REFUTED sentence the HIGH rested
on, and the pin-count argument is about how hard it is to fix, not how bad it is. Note the
mechanism's origin is now measured: `/dawnspur-heat/` is the board the timer comes from
(entry #16, row two).

**Cost of getting it wrong.** A label, only — this is the cheapest call on the list.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** MEDIUM. The wait row's
adjudication in test/lexicon-ledger.js is set to MEDIUM, and its by: field is re-sourced in
the same edit: it currently reads 'audit section 1.3', which rules neither word. The row's
heat drives block and mutates:true are not touched. By counsel on David's instruction,
2026-09-09. *(unanimous; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one moderated
debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in this block
are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named under Carried out moved some
of them.)*

- *Why:* UNCHANGED on the check, which re-ran the safety argument rather than reading it;
  the action's line pointers are corrected here because the first draft's range excluded the
  very field it tells the orchestrator to re-source. Unanimous, and the record is internally
  inconsistent today in a way that MEDIUM closes. test/lexicon-ledger.js:159 ships the row as
  adjudication HIGH and :160 sources it to 'audit section 1.3', while the SAME object's desk
  sentence at :162 states the refutation the HIGH rested on: 'WARM is what ends the sitting:
  banked darkens every job the instant commitWarm() returns (litJobs() is [] before any
  wait).' The audit's own disposition at docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:838-842
  rules nothing — it records the reviewer's MEDIUM on the report's own grounds and says HIGH
  is defensible only if the pin count dominates, and closes 'Both readings are recorded; the
  guard pins the mechanism regardless of the label.' So the file publishes a severity its
  cited source does not give and its own text undercuts. The surviving HIGH argument — twelve
  test files freeze heat's bytes, so the divergence can never be conformed — prices remedy
  difficulty, and no sentence in the audit says severity measures remedy difficulty; where
  the audit does gloss a severity (:238) it grades propagation harm. Canon also disposes of
  the board: 'The heat sitting (/dawnspur-heat/, passed) collapsed the triangle into a phase
  lock (R10) but was sat and passed as a harness step; it stands as played'
  (docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:125-127) — a severity resting on 'it can never be
  conformed' re-litigates a disposition canon made. The timer's only visible effect is the
  documented design (beat :19-23).
- *Dissent:* HIGH on the freeze count — twelve test files mean the divergence can only ever
  be renamed on the other eleven boards, so MEDIUM understates permanence. It lost because
  permanence is not harm and the audit nowhere defines severity to include it. The cost lens
  raised the one real hazard of downgrading and it was measured and cleared: MEDIUM is in
  RANKS at test/lexicon.test.js:608 so the downgrade cannot trip the vocabulary gate; the
  'HIGH means measured' sweep at :300-312 skips any row that is not HIGH or CONTESTED (:304),
  so a downgrade does drop wait out of that sweep — but wait carries its own STATIC test at
  test/lexicon.test.js:493-527 which is not adjudication-gated and which demands mutates
  declared on every board and a driven arming path wherever mutates is true. That is what
  makes MEDIUM safe here, and it is a constraint on carrying the ruling out, not a footnote.
  The skeptic's second reservation is carried and was omitted from the first draft: ruling
  this one word without ruling the TAXONOMY leaves the next contested severity (entry 14,
  armed) to re-argue the same question with no principle settled — so the ledger row must say
  this is a label call on one row and must not be cited as a severity taxonomy.
- *For David, not blocking:* Nothing. Both labels are one field in a guard file; no board
  byte, no signed section and no pin moves. The ledger's own header states the edit is always
  in the guard and never in a board.
- *Action:* [test] In test/lexicon-ledger.js at the wait row, set adjudication (:159) to
  MEDIUM and change by: (:160) from 'audit section 1.3' to this counsel ruling with its date
  — the audit rules neither word, so leaving the old attribution would credit a ruling to a
  source that did not make it. Do NOT remove or narrow the heat entry's mutates:true or its
  drives block: MEDIUM drops the row out of the general HIGH-means-measured gate at
  test/lexicon.test.js:300-312, and the bespoke static test at :493-527 is the only thing
  then pinning the arming path. (C:/dev/skyrail-boards/test/lexicon-ledger.js) · [record]
  Record the ruling and, beside the label, two things that survive it: (1) the hazard — an
  auditor who extends the walk-string DSL to heat drives the board to its terminal state and
  can read the resulting true as a world event (docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:188,
  the 'Wrong action' bullet); MEDIUM is not 'safe to extend the DSL', the row and its guard
  carry that, not the word. (2) That this ruling settles one row's label and is not a
  severity taxonomy — entry 14 (armed) is argued on its own facts.
  (C:/dev/skyrail-boards/docs/decisions-open-2026-09-02.md)
- *Carried out:* at `1cf0bba` — `test/lexicon-ledger.js`.

## 31. The lineage membership declaration

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: a yes/no.)*
Yes or no: confirm the orchestrator's 2026-09-01 ruling that dawnspur-site and
dawnspur-halt belong to the city lineage, not the desk, for the purpose of the
name-crossing check?

- *Why:* The check counts names shared between the two lineages, and which boards belong to
  which lineage changed that count from 5 to 22 to 31 across three attempts to infer it from
  the boards' own headers — so the membership is now written down as a ruling rather than
  guessed. Halt is the ancestor every city board copies its interface from, and site shares
  most of its names with halt, so putting either on the desk side would turn their harmless
  look-alike names into "crossings" and bury the real ones.
- *Options:*
  a. **Yes — city side** — Site and halt stay declared as city boards. *Costs:* One name,
     litJobs, counts as a crossing, only because site carries it alongside four desk boards
     (heat, scale, line and storm).
  b. **No — desk side** — One or both move to the desk list. *Costs:* Their look-alike names
     with the city boards (sited, scaffold, bill, panes, rim and more) read as crossings and
     bury the real ones.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* Orchestrator's read: confirm it — the count
  moving 5/22/31 with the choice is exactly why it must be declared, and one crossing name is
  the smallest cost of the placements tried. *Yours:* The confirmation. The ruling was the
  orchestrator's; the audit carries it as declared, not blocked.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked and the check is green today. If the placement is
  wrong, the crossing count grades the wrong surface and real crossings hide among harmless
  twins.

**Question.** Yes or no: confirm the orchestrator's 2026-09-01 ruling that `dawnspur-site`
and `dawnspur-halt` sit on the **city** side?

INFERRED. Three sweeps inferred three memberships from the sims' headers and the seam count

placement is one row — `litJobs` — which crosses only because site carries it beside heat,
scale, line and storm."*

**What source says.** `docs/name-collisions-audit-2026-09-01.md:799`: *"The rulings it carries
as **declared, not blocked**: `armed`; the membership."*

**Blocked.** Nothing — declared, not blocked, and the guard is green at HEAD.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Confirm it.* The seam count moving 5/22/31 with the choice is
exactly why it must be declared rather than inferred, and the stated cost of one crossing
row is the smallest of the three options.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** The name-collision guard's seam count grades the wrong
surface, and real crossings get buried among benign twins.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** CONFIRMED. dawnspur-site and
dawnspur-halt stay declared on the CITY side of the name-crossing check. The confirmation
is recorded as by counsel on David's instruction, 2026-09-09; the ledger comment's existing
attribution — 'The lists below are the orchestrator's ruling, 2026-09-01' — is not
rewritten, and nothing is recorded in David's name. *(unanimous; four lenses — canon, cost,
the reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not
David's words. Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the
landing named under Carried out moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* UNCHANGED — the check found no defect and reproduced the green claim exactly (node

## 32. Canon §5's `(d)` gloss (optional)

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: pick one of two or three.)*
In the signed section 5 of the rulebook, the answer reads "(d) now, (c) next" but the
question above it letters only three choices — (a) the run, (b) the in-progress purchase,
(c) the reserve-and-storm — and then adds "or none this sitting" without a letter; does
"(d)" mean "none of the three stakes" or "the reserve without the storm"?

- *Why:* A reader cannot resolve "(d)" from the file, because no choice carries that
  letter; the nearest candidate, the unlettered "none this sitting", conflicts with the scale
  recut that shipped, which carries the reserve. The section is signed, so adding the
  one-line gloss is yours; the sweep marked the item optional.
- *Options:*
  a. **"None of the three stakes"** — The scale sitting carries no stake at all — the
     question's unlettered fourth choice. *Costs:* One line added to a signed section, and it
     contradicts the shipped scale recut, which carries the reserve.
  b. **"The reserve without the storm"** — The scale sitting carries the visible reserve,
     with the storm deferred to the next sitting. *Costs:* One line added to a signed section.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* Orchestrator's read: "the reserve without the
  storm" — the shipped recut carries the reserve, which rules out "none"; but the section is
  signed and the gloss is David's. *Yours:* The gloss itself, because section 5 is signed.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked. One line of a signed section stays unreadable.

**Question.** Pick one: does *"(d)"* mean *"none of the three stakes"*, or *"the reserve
without the storm"*?

**What the document says.** `docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:211` (the sweep cited `:158`;
**drifted by 12 lines**): *"**Proposed answer: (d) now, (c) next — and (a) and (b) arrive
INSIDE the storm sitting as what a storm does, never as standalone mechanics.**"* The

carries the reserve, so '(d)' cannot mean 'none'. … One line; §5 is SIGNED, so glossing it

**Orchestrator's read.** *"The reserve without the storm"* — the shipped recut carries the
reserve, which rules out "none"; but it is a signed section and the gloss is yours.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** One line of a SIGNED section stays unreadable.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** '(d)' means THE RESERVE
WITHOUT THE STORM — specifically, in canon's own signed words, 'pure build plus the visible
reserve and the tending run, no deaths', with the storm as the next sitting's reckoning.
This is a reading of a SIGNED section, which the council may state; it is NOT written into
the body of section 5. The gloss is recorded in the decisions ledger, and if any line is
placed near section 5 it is a dated bracketed annotation that QUOTES the section's own
signed preamble and is attributed to counsel on David's instruction, 2026-09-09 — never
phrased in David's voice, never folded into the signed answer sentence. *(unanimous; four
lenses — canon, cost, the reader, the skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial
check, one revision. Not David's words. Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree
the council read; the landing named under Carried out moved some of them.)*

- *Why:* Disposition UNCHANGED; the check passed on authority and cites, and its one
  correction to the STRENGTH of the claim is adopted, because it is right on the record.
  Unanimous. The entry says a reader cannot resolve (d) from the file. Verified at HEAD,
  docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:161-165 is the signature block four lines above the
  question: 'SIGNED — David, 2026-08-25: "section 5 is signed." The staged answer below is
  the ruling: the scale recut ships pure build plus the visible reserve and the tending run,
  no deaths; the storm is the next sitting's reckoning; haul and in-progress stakes arrive
  inside the storm.' The question at :167-168 letters (a) the run, (b) the in-progress
  purchase, (c) the reserve-and-storm, and adds 'or none this sitting' unlettered; the
  proposed answer at :170 reads '(d) now, (c) next'; evidence item 1 at :173-175 says the
  same thing again. CORRECTION to the first draft, which overstated: the preamble LETTERS
  NOTHING, so identifying its content with '(d)' is an inference, not a restatement — a
  strong one, since the preamble ships the reserve-and-tending now with the storm next and
  (c) at :167-168 is the reserve-and-storm, but an inference. The honest gloss, and the
  wording to record, is 'the only reading of (d) consistent with the section's own signed

  reserve (docs/sweep-2026-09-02.md:534-537). Authority is unaffected either way: choosing
  between readings of a signed section is expressly the council's.
- *Dissent:* The canon lens noted that if David reads the preamble as sufficient, the gloss
  is redundant and 'no change' is the better answer. That is not a dissent on the reading; it
  is absorbed by recording the gloss in the ledger rather than in canon. The reader lens's
  sharpest counter is now carried in the rationale rather than buried here: 'I may be
  over-reading the banner — it names the recut's contents, not the letter.' It is correct
  that the banner letters nothing, and the ruling's language has been weakened to match. The
  sharper delivery constraint, raised by the skeptic and agreed by all four, stands: adding a
  new explanatory sentence into the body of a section David signed, in that section's voice,

  than closed: because the council declines option b's stated delivery (a line added to the
  signed section) and returns it, the entry's cost of getting it wrong — one line of a SIGNED
  section stays unreadable to a reader of canon — is closed for a reader of the LEDGER and
  not for a reader of canon until David answers.
- *For David, not blocking:* Only if he wants the letter itself repaired inside the signed
  sentence. Exact question: 'Section 5 letters three options — (a) the run, (b) the
  in-progress purchase, (c) the reserve-and-storm — plus an unlettered "or none this
  sitting", and then answers (d), which the section never defines. Your own signature banner
  at docs/mechanisms-recommitted.md:161-165 says what the sitting ships — pure build plus the
  visible reserve and the tending run, no deaths, storm next — which is the only reading of

## 33. `KILLS.md` ordering (conditional on #7)

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: pick one of two or three.)*
If, under decision 7, the five passed sittings get entries in the kills ledger, should they
run newest-first or be appended oldest-last?

- *Why:* The ledger's two sections disagree with each other: the pre-host section runs
  oldest-first and the hosted era runs newest-first. The three new kill entries have since
  landed newest-first, so for kills the order is settled by events and only the passes' order
  is still open.
- *Options:*
  a. **Newest first** — The pass entries follow the hosted era's existing order. *Costs:*
     None named; matches what the file already does.
  b. **Oldest last** — The pass entries are appended in date order. *Costs:* The anchors
     change, not the text.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* Orchestrator's read: newest first — already the
  hosted era's observed order, and now reinforced by a landing. *Yours:* The order — and only
  if you answer yes to decision 7.
- *If undecided:* Only the pass entries' anchors, and only if decision 7 is yes. Getting it
  wrong means re-cutting anchors; no substantive loss.

**Question.** If the passes land: descending (newest first), or appended oldest-last?

**What the document says.** `C:\tmp\kills-proposal\KILLS-proposal.md:49-53`: *"`KILLS.md`'s
two sections disagree about order: the pre-host section runs oldest-first, the hosted era
runs newest-first … If you want it appended oldest-last instead, say so and the anchors
change, not the text."*

**What source says.** **Effectively settled for the kills by events** — `b95a9c9` landed
the three new entries descending, and `KILLS.md` at HEAD runs 2026-08-31, 08-30, 08-30,
08-30, 08-28/29, 08-25, 08-24, 08-24. Only the passes are still open.

**Blocked.** Only the pass anchors, and only if #7 is yes.

**Orchestrator's read.** *Descending.* Already the hosted era's observed order and now
reinforced by a landing.

**Cost of getting it wrong.** Re-cut anchors; no substantive loss.

**RULED — by counsel on David's instruction of 2026-09-09.** DESCENDING — newest first,
ordered by MERGE INSTANT, with that key written into the heading so a reader does not take
position for sit order. Existing rows are not reordered: the oldest-first pre-host section
and the ascending 2026-08-25 seed pair stay exactly as they are. The check confirmed the
choice and both riders and refuted the de-lining action's published COUNT; that action is
rewritten with the measured figure and split three ways, and the confidence label is
corrected from unanimous to majority. *(majority; four lenses — canon, cost, the reader,
the skeptic — one moderated debate, one adversarial check, one revision. Not David's words.
Line-cites in this block are at `e226a9f`, the tree the council read; the landing named
under Carried out moved some of them.)*

## 34. Five note-only calls, each with a default already proposed

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: read one table first.)*
Do you take the record's proposed default on each of five one-line wording points in passed
beats, or does any of them need a different answer?

- *Why:* Each is a wording call that two readers have already looked at, none changes a
  measured number, and none blocks anything, so they are grouped to be cleared in one pass.
  The beats they sit in are already passed and frozen.
- *Options:*
  a. **Herbs beat: "Put them up. Consist is already home with the herbs"** — Treat
     "inherited, not replayed" as a note only — no rewrite. *Costs:* Note only; nothing measured
     changes.
  b. **Herbs beat: "Player action: tap to collect."** — The sentence stands; the later
     they-remember beat records the split. *Costs:* Note only; nothing measured changes.
  c. **They-remember beat: "Mosswake is where the light is"** — Read as figurative; the
     board's actual bytes govern what is lit. *Costs:* Note only; nothing measured changes.
  d. **Two-ways beat: "64 for 14, stake 0"** — Leave it. It appears twice in the beat, not
     once. *Costs:* Note only; nothing measured changes.
  e. **Storm beat: "Canon §7 forbids it by name"** — The record lists this citation itself as
     the point for you and proposes no default for it — the entry's words are "the citation
     itself is the question". *Costs:* Note only; but see below — no default is stated for this
     row.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* Orchestrator's read: take the record's default
  on all five — each was reached by two readers and none changes a measured number. *Yours:*
  Each of the five wording calls, since the beats are passed and the words are yours.
- *If undecided:* Nothing is blocked for any of the five. What stays wrong is wording drift
  in beats that are already passed and frozen.
- *What the entry does not say:* The entry says each of the five has a default already
  proposed, but for the fifth ("Canon §7 forbids it by name") the record's proposal reads
  "the citation itself is the question", which is a question rather than a default, and the
  entry does not say what about the citation is in question. The card cannot say what taking
  "the default" means for that row.

Grouped because each is a one-line register call, the record already states the
recommendation, and none blocks anything. All five line numbers re-derived at HEAD.

## 43. The desk voice in three passed city sims — pinned evidence to leave, or a re-voicing owed before a fourth board inherits it?

**In plain terms.** *(2026-09-09, from a plain-language pass checked twice against this entry; effort: pick one of two or three.)*
Three passed city boards carry the sentence "The train brought the Mosswake cargo home." —
desk voice, never shown to a player, quoted in no document — and one board's test freezes
it word for word; is that dead prose to leave alone as passed evidence, or is a re-voicing
owed before a fourth city board copies it?

- *Why:* Nothing renders the sentence and no document quotes it; the next city board, if it
  is cut by copying the last passed one, would inherit it a fourth time, and the site board
  carries three more unrendered desk-voiced sentences of its own. A rule forbidding the
  sentence would be red on three passed boards today and could only go green by editing three
  boards and one test.
- *Options:*
  a. **Leave it as pinned evidence** — The three boards and the test stay as they are.
     *Costs:* The next city board inherits the sentence a fourth time, and the first city board
     to actually show its run sentence shows the desk's voice.
  b. **Re-voice before the fourth board** — Rewrite the sentence in the city's voice on the
     three boards and update the test that freezes it. *Costs:* Edits to three passed, frozen
     boards plus the one test that freezes the sentence.
  c. **Middle path (the orchestrator's): leave the three, forbid the fourth** — The three
     passed boards keep the sentence; a rule forbids it only on boards that pass after today.
     *Costs:* That scoping is a convention David would be setting, which is why it is listed
     rather than built.
- *The orchestrator's read, not a ruling:* Orchestrator's read: leave the three and forbid
  the fourth — a rule that is red on passed bytes is the permanently-red gate the project
  warns of, while a rule scoped to boards passing after today ratchets. *Yours:* The scoping
  convention — whether a rule may apply only to boards that pass from now on.
- *If undecided:* Nothing today. The next city board, if cut by copying the last passed
  one, inherits the line.
